\displaystyle\times\prod_{i:1\leq i\leq\lambda_{i}^{\prime}}\prod_{j=1}^{i-1}q^{N+\lambda_{i}^{\prime}-i}_{N-i+j}\cdot\prod_{i:\lambda_{i}^{\prime}<i\leq\ell(\lambda^{\prime})}\prod_{j=1}^{\lambda_{i}^{\prime}}q_{N-i+j}^{N+\lambda_{i}^{\prime}-i}

\displaystyle\times\left(q_{-N}^{\mu^{c}_{1}}\right)^{-1}\cdot\prod_{i:2\leq i\leq\mu^{c}_{i}+1}\prod_{j=1}^{i-2}q_{i-j-N-1}^{\mu^{c}_{i}-j}\cdot\prod_{i:\mu^{c}_{i}+1<i\leq\ell(\mu^{c})}\prod_{j=1}^{\mu^{c}_{i}}q_{i-j-N-1}^{\mu^{c}_{i}-j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q^{\varpi_{1}(N-1,-1,-1)}\cdot q^{\varpi_{2}(\lambda^{\prime},N,0)}\cdot\overline{q^{\varpi_{2}(\mu^{c},N-1,2)}}\cdot q^{\varpi_{3}(\nu^{r},N,0)}\cdot q^{\varpi_{4}(\lambda^{\prime},N,1)}\cdot q^{\varpi_{7}(\mu^{c},N)}.

Now we have

\displaystyle\frac{q^{\widetilde{\omega}_{\min}(\lambda,\mu^{rc},\nu;N)}\cdot q^{\widetilde{\omega}_{\min}(\lambda,\mu,\nu^{rc};N)}}{q^{\widetilde{\omega}_{\min}(\lambda,\mu,\nu;N)}\cdot q^{\widetilde{\omega}_{\min}(\lambda,\mu^{rc},\nu^{rc};N)}}=1

and still demand the following lemmas to achieve the recurrence (
9
).

Lemma 4.33
.

For any partitions
\mu,\nu\neq\emptyset
and
\lambda
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{q^{\widetilde{\omega}_{\min}^{LD}(\lambda,\mu^{r},\nu^{c};n)}\cdot q^{\widetilde{\omega}_{\min}^{RU}(\lambda,\mu^{c},\nu^{r};n)}}{q^{\widetilde{\omega}_{\min}(\lambda,\mu,\nu;n)}\cdot q^{\widetilde{\omega}_{\min}(\lambda,\mu^{rc},\nu^{rc};n)}}=q^{K_{3}(\lambda,\mu,\nu)}

where

\displaystyle q^{K_{3}(\lambda,\mu,\nu)}:=q_{d(\mu)-1}^{-\mu_{d(\mu)}}\cdot\prod\limits_{i=1}^{d(\mu)-1}q_{i}\cdot\prod\limits_{i=1}^{d(\mu)-1}\left(\frac{q_{i}}{q_{i-1}}\right)^{\mu_{i}}\cdot\prod\limits_{i=1}^{\ell(\lambda^{\prime})}\left(\frac{q_{-i}}{q_{-i+1}}\right)^{\lambda^{\prime}_{i}}\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{\widetilde{d}(\nu)-d(\nu)}q_{-i}^{-1}

is independent of
N
.

Proof.

It follows from Lemmas
4.14
,
4.31
,
4.32
,
4.34
,
4.29
,
4.28
,
4.35
,
4.23
. ∎

As in Section 4.3.2, we have

Lemma 4.34
.

\displaystyle\frac{q^{\varpi_{1}(N-1,1,1)}\cdot q^{\varpi_{1}(N-1,-1,-1)}}{\left(q^{\varpi_{1}(N-1,0,0)}\right)^{2}}=q_{0}^{1-N}\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{N-1}q_{-i}.

Lemma 4.35
.

For any partition
\eta\neq\emptyset
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{q^{\varpi_{3}(\eta^{c},N,2)}q^{\varpi_{3}(\eta^{r},N,0)}}{q^{\varpi_{3}(\eta,N,1)}q^{\varpi_{3}(\eta^{rc},N,1)}}=q_{0}^{N}\cdot\prod_{i=0}^{\widetilde{d}(\eta)-d(\eta)}q_{-i}^{-1}.

5.
Orbifold PT theory and the graphical condensation recurrence

We recall the essential idea in [
14
, Section 4] that the PT vertex can be alternatively expressed as the sum over certain subset of AB configurations, which is available for deriving the graphical condensation recurrence in double-dimer model [
13
] for PT theory. In this section, we extend this idea to the PT
\mathbb{Z}_{n}
-vertex with the similar double-dimer model description, and obtain three recurrences as in the orbifold DT case one of which generalizes the recurrence in [
14
, Section 4.5].

5.1.
Labelled box configurations and AB configurations for orbifold PT theory

We will briefly review the relation between labelled box configurations with AB configurations in [
14
, Section 4.2], and then show the PT
\mathbb{Z}_{n}
-vertex as a sum indexed by some AB configurations. In the following we will employ notations
\mathrm{I}^{-},\mathrm{II},\mathrm{III}
defined with respect to
(\lambda,\mu,\nu)
in Section 3.

Definition 5.1
.

([
14
, Definition 4.2.1 and Conditions 4.2.2, 4.2.3])

(1)
An AB configuration is a pair
(A,B)
of finite sets where
A\subseteq\mathrm{I}^{-}\cup\mathrm{III}
and
B\subseteq\mathrm{II}\cup\mathrm{III}
has elements called boxes, satisfying the following conditions

(i)
if
w\in\mathrm{I}^{-}\cup\mathrm{III}
and any of

\displaystyle(w_{1}-1,w_{2},w_{3}),\;\;(w_{1},w_{2}-1,w_{3}),\;\;(w_{1},w_{2},w_{3}-1)

is a box in
A
, then
w\in A
.

(ii)
if
w\in\mathrm{II}\cup\mathrm{III}
and any of

\displaystyle(w_{1}-1,w_{2},w_{3}),\;\;(w_{1},w_{2}-1,w_{3}),\;\;(w_{1},w_{2},w_{3}-1)

is a box in
B
, then
w\in B
.

We denote by
\mathcal{AB}_{all}
the set of all AB configurations.

(2)
Given a labelled box configuration
\widetilde{\pi}
with outgoing partitions
(\lambda,\mu,\nu)
. If an AB configuration
(A,B)
satisfies

A\cup B=\widetilde{\pi}
and
A\cap B=\mathrm{III}_{ulb}(\widetilde{\pi})
as the same sets of boxes in
\widetilde{\pi}
, then we call
(A,B)
an AB configuration on
\widetilde{\pi}
.
We denote by
\mathcal{AB}(\widetilde{\pi})
the set of all AB configurations on
\widetilde{\pi}
.

Let
\widehat{\mathcal{P}}(\lambda,\mu,\nu)
be the set of all labelled box configurations with outgoing partitions
(\lambda,\mu,\nu)
. Given
\widetilde{\pi}\in\widehat{\mathcal{P}}(\lambda,\mu,\nu)
, one can construct the base AB configuration
AB_{base}(\widetilde{\pi})=(A,B)
in [
14
, Definition 4.2.4] where
A:=\mathrm{I}^{-}(\widetilde{\pi})\cup\mathrm{III}(\widetilde{\pi})
and
B:=\mathrm{II}(\widetilde{\pi})\cup\mathrm{III}_{ulb}(\widetilde{\pi})
. It is shown in [
14
, Section 4.2.1] that
AB_{base}(\widetilde{\pi})\in\mathcal{AB}(\widetilde{\pi})
. Conversely,
(A,B)\in\mathcal{AB}_{all}
is called a labelled AB configuration if the algorithm [
14
, Algorithm 4.2.13] succeeds in labelling connected components of
\mathcal{L}(A,B):=(\mathrm{I}^{-}\cap A)\cup(\mathrm{II}\setminus B)\cup(\mathrm{III}\cap(A\Delta B))
. For a labelled AB configuration
(A,B)
, the labelled boxes
\widetilde{\pi}(A,B)
is defined in [
14
, Definition 4.2.22].
Due to [
14
, Lemma 4.2.24], we define

\displaystyle\mathcal{AB}:=\bigcup_{\widetilde{\pi}\in\widehat{\mathcal{P}}(\lambda,\mu,\nu)}\mathcal{AB}(\widetilde{\pi}).

Then
(A,B)\in\mathcal{AB}
if and only if
(A,B)
is a labelled AB configuration by [
14
, Theorem 4.2.26] and
\widetilde{\pi}(A,B)
is a labelled box configuration if
(A,B)\in\mathcal{AB}
by [
14
, Corollary 4.2.27].
Recall that
\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}(\lambda,\mu,\nu)
is the set of components of the moduli space of labelled box configurations with outgoing partitions
(\lambda,\mu,\nu)
in Section 3.
By [
14
, Corollary 4.2.31], if
[\widetilde{\pi}_{1}],[\widetilde{\pi}_{2}]\in\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}(\lambda,\mu,\nu)
and
[\widetilde{\pi}_{1}]\neq[\widetilde{\pi}_{2}]
, then
\mathcal{AB}(\widetilde{\pi}_{1})\cap\mathcal{AB}(\widetilde{\pi}_{2})=\emptyset
. For any element
\widetilde{\pi}\in\widehat{\mathcal{P}}(\lambda,\mu,\nu)
in one given component in
\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}(\lambda,\mu,\nu)
, the sets
\mathcal{AB}(\widetilde{\pi})
are the same by the definition. Then

\displaystyle\mathcal{AB}:=\bigcup_{[\widetilde{\pi}]\in\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}(\lambda,\mu,\nu)}\mathcal{AB}(\widetilde{\pi}).

And it is shown in [
14
, Corollary 4.2.34] that
|\mathcal{AB}(\widetilde{\pi})|=\chi_{\mathrm{top}}([\widetilde{\pi}])

where
[\widetilde{\pi}]\in\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}(\lambda,\mu,\nu)
is the component of the moduli space of labellings of
\underline{\widetilde{\pi}}
containing
\widetilde{\pi}
. The orbifold version of [
14
, Definition 4.2.35] is presented as follows.

Definition 5.2
.

\displaystyle Z_{\mathcal{AB}}(q_{0},q_{1},\cdots,q_{n-1})=\sum_{(A,B)\in\mathcal{AB}}\prod_{l=0}^{n-1}q_{l}^{|A|_{l}+|B|_{l}-|\mathrm{II}|_{l}-2|\mathrm{III}|_{l}}.

This definition is an alternative expression of the orbifold PT topological vertex
W^{n}_{\lambda\mu\nu}
, i.e., we have the following orbifold version of [
14
, Theorem 4.2.36].

Theorem 5.3
.

\displaystyle W^{n}_{\lambda\mu\nu}(q_{0},q_{1},\cdots,q_{n-1})=Z_{\mathcal{AB}}(q_{0},q_{1},\cdots,q_{n-1}).

Proof.

With the discussion above, we have

\displaystyle Z_{\mathcal{AB}}(q_{0},q_{1},\cdots,q_{n-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{(A,B)\in\mathcal{AB}}\prod_{l=0}^{n-1}q_{l}^{|A|_{l}+|B|_{l}-|\mathrm{II}|_{l}-2|\mathrm{III}|_{l}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{[\widetilde{\pi}]\in\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}(\lambda,\mu,\nu)}\sum_{(A,B)\in\mathcal{AB}(\widetilde{\pi})}\prod_{l=0}^{n-1}q_{l}^{|A\cup B|_{l}+|A\cap B|_{l}-|\mathrm{II}|_{l}-2|\mathrm{III}|_{l}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{[\widetilde{\pi}]\in\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}(\lambda,\mu,\nu)}\sum_{(A,B)\in\mathcal{AB}(\widetilde{\pi})}\prod_{l=0}^{n-1}q_{l}^{\ell(\widetilde{\pi})_{l}+\|\pi_{\mathrm{min}}(\lambda,\mu,\nu)\|_{l}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{[\widetilde{\pi}]\in\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}(\lambda,\mu,\nu)}|\mathcal{AB}(\widetilde{\pi})|\prod_{l=0}^{n-1}q_{l}^{\ell(\widetilde{\pi})_{l}+\|\pi_{\mathrm{min}}(\lambda,\mu,\nu)\|_{l}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{[\widetilde{\pi}]\in\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}(\lambda,\mu,\nu)}\chi_{\mathrm{top}}([\widetilde{\pi}])\prod_{l=0}^{n-1}q_{l}^{\ell(\widetilde{\pi})_{l}+\|\pi_{\mathrm{min}}(\lambda,\mu,\nu)\|_{l}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle W^{n}_{\lambda\mu\nu}(q_{0},q_{1},\cdots,q_{n-1}).

∎

5.2.
The double-dimer model for orbifold PT theory

We will briefly review the (labelled) double-dimer model introduced in [
14
, Section 4.3 and 4.4], which is also valid for describing the PT
\mathbb{Z}_{n}
-vertex.
Given an AB configuration
(A,B)
, define

\displaystyle\mathfrak{A}=R_{2}\cup(\mathrm{I}^{-}\cup\mathrm{III})\setminus A\;\;\;\;\mbox{and}\;\;\;\;\mathfrak{B}=R_{1}\cup(\mathrm{II}\cup\mathrm{III})\setminus B

where
R_{1}
(resp.
R_{2}
) is the subset of
\mathbb{Z}^{3}
whose elements have at least one negative coordinate (resp. two negative coordinates). One can view
\mathfrak{A}
and
\mathfrak{B}
as rhombus tilings of the plane due to [
14
, Lemma 4.3.3], which are corresponding to dimer configurations
\mathbf{D}_{A}
and
\mathbf{D}_{B}
of the infinite honeycomb graph
\mathbf{H}
. A double-dimer configuration is obtained from the union of two dimer configurations. Let
\mathbf{D}_{(A,B)}
be the double-dimer configuration on
\mathbf{H}
by superimposing
\mathbf{D}_{A}
and
\mathbf{D}_{B}
with their origins in
\mathbb{Z}^{3}
corresponding to the same face of
\mathbf{H}
. Then
\mathbf{D}_{(A,B)}
is composed of loops, double edges and infinite paths. One can refer to [
14
, Figure 9 and 10] for examples. Given an AB configuration
(A,B)
, there is a labelling algorithm [
14
, Algorithm 4.3.11] for
\mathbf{D}_{(A,B)}
, which is shown to be equivalent to [
14
, Algorithm 4.2.13] in the sense that
(A,B)\in\mathcal{AB}
if and only if Algorithm 4.3.11 in [
14
] succeeds (see [
14
, Theorem 4.4.10]), and for a given
(A,B)\in\mathcal{AB}
, the labelling in [
14
, Algorithm 4.2.13] assigned for some cell is the same as the one in [
14
, Algorithm 4.3.11] for the corresponding face (see [
14
, Theorem 4.4.11]). And it is verified in [
14
, Theorem 4.4.12] that
(A,B)\in\mathcal{AB}
if and only if both ends of each path in
\mathbf{D}_{(A,B)}
are contained in sector
i
for some
i\in\{1,2,3\}
. To describe the orbifold PT vertex and compare with the orbifold DT case, one need to truncate
\mathbf{D}_{(A,B)}
on
\mathbf{H}
to be the one on
\mathbf{H}(N)
. The following definition is useful for describing the truncation.

Definition 5.4
.

([
14
, Definition 4.4.13 and 4.4.15])
Let
\mathbf{G}=(\mathbf{V}_{1},\mathbf{V}_{2},\mathbf{E})
be a finite edge-weighted bipartite planar graph with
|\mathbf{V}_{1}|=|\mathbf{V}_{2}|
. Let
\mathbf{N}
be a set of special vertices called nodes on the outer face of
\mathbf{G}
.
A double-dimer configuration on
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{N})
is a multiset of the edges of
\mathbf{G}
such that each internal vertex is the endpoint of exactly two edges and each vertex in
\mathbf{N}
is the endpoint of exactly one edge. A planar pairing
\sigma
is tripartite if the nodes can be divided into three circularly contiguous sets
R
,
G
and
B
(the nodes in
R
,
G
and
B
are often colored by red, green and blue respectively) such that no node is paired with a node in the same set.

For
N
large enough, it is shown that
\mathbf{D}_{B}
should be truncated to be a perfect matching
\mathbf{D}_{B}(N)
of
\mathbf{H}(N)
by [
14
, Lemma 4.4.14] and
\mathbf{D}_{A}
could be truncated to be a partial matching
\mathbf{D}_{A}(N)
of
\mathbf{H}(N)
, which implies that
\mathbf{D}_{(A,B)}
can be truncated to be a double-dimer configuration
\mathbf{D}_{(A,B)}(N)
on
\mathbf{H}(N)
(see [
14
, Figure 11] for example) with nodes. By [
14
, Theorem 4.4.17], if
(A,B)\in\mathcal{AB}
and
N
is large enough, the double-dimer configuration
\mathbf{D}_{(A,B)}(N)
is tripartite with nodes explicitly described as follows. Let
S_{1}=S(\lambda)
,
S_{2}=S(\mu)
and
S_{3}=S(\nu)
be the Maya diagrams of
\lambda,\mu,\nu
respectively. Define
\mathbf{N}_{i}^{+}(N)
(resp.
\mathbf{N}_{i}^{-}(N)
) to be the set of vertices on the outer face of
\mathbf{H}(N)
in sector
i^{+}
(resp. in sector
i^{-}
) which are not labelled by any of elements of
S_{i}^{+}
(resp.
S_{i}^{-}
). Set
\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu,\nu}(N)=\bigcup\limits_{i=1}^{3}\mathbf{N}_{i}^{+}(N)\cup\mathbf{N}_{i}^{-}(N)
. It is proved in [
14
, Corollary 4.4.22] that the set of nodes of
\mathbf{D}_{(A,B)}(N)
in sector
i
is
\mathbf{N}_{i}=\mathbf{N}_{i}^{+}(N)\cup\mathbf{N}_{i}^{-}(N)
. We have the following color scheme [
14
] for the nodes of
\mathbf{D}_{(A,B)}(N)
. In sector 1, the nodes in
N_{1}^{+}
are colored by blue and the nodes in
N_{1}^{-}
are colored by red; in sector 2, the nodes in
N_{2}^{+}
are colored by red and the nodes in
N_{2}^{-}
are colored by green; in sector 3, the nodes in
N_{3}^{+}
are colored by green and the nodes in
N_{3}^{-}
are colored by blue. Since
|\mathbf{N}_{i}^{+}(N)|=|\mathbf{N}_{i}^{-}(N)|
, one can equip nodes
\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu,\nu}(N)
with the following tripartite rainbow pairing.

Definition 5.5
.

([
14
, Section 4.4])
Suppose there are
2r
nodes in sector
i
numbered consecutively in clockwise order by
1,2,\cdots,2r
, which are vertices on the outer face of
\mathbf{H}(N)
. If
r>0
, we call the pairing
((1,2r),(2,2r-1),\cdots,(r,r+1))
the rainbow pairing of the nodes in sector
i
. If
r=0
, we call the empty pairing as the rainbow pairing of the nodes in sector
i
. Denote the nodes by
\mathbf{N}
. If the nodes in sector
i
are paired according to the rainbow pairing for every
1\leq i\leq 3
, we call the resulting pairing of
\mathbf{N}
the rainbow pairing of
\mathbf{N}
.

Denote by
\mathbf{D}_{\sigma}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{N})
the set of all double-dimer configurations on
\mathbf{G}
with nodes
\mathbf{N}
subject to the rainbow pairing
\sigma
. Let
\mathbf{Z}^{DD}_{\sigma}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{N})
be the sum of all the weights of double-dimer configurations in
\mathbf{D}_{\sigma}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{N})
, where the weight of a
k
-loop double-dimer configuration is
2^{k}
times its edge-weight defined by the product of weights of all its edges.

It is shown before [
14
, Definition 4.4.23] that a given AB configuration
(A,B)
can be consecutively changed into an AB configuration
(\mathrm{III},\mathrm{II}\cup\mathrm{III})\in\mathcal{AB}(\widetilde{\pi})
by a sequence of removing or adding a single box, where
\widetilde{\pi}
is some labelled box configuration which is composed of type
\mathrm{II}
boxes and type
\mathrm{III}
boxes which are unlabelled. If an AB configuration
(A^{\prime},B^{\prime})
is derived from an AB configuration
(A,B)
by adding (resp. removing) a box to (resp. from)
A
or
B
and all the boxes in
A^{\prime}\cup B^{\prime}
and
A\cup B
are corresponding to faces of
\mathbf{H}(N)
, then
\mathbf{D}_{A^{\prime}}(N)
or
\mathbf{D}_{B^{\prime}}(N)
is obtained from
\mathbf{D}_{A}(N)
or
\mathbf{D}_{B}(N)
by a local move shown in [
14
, Figure 13] (resp. [
14
, Figure 12]). According to the weighted rule in Definition
4.3
, removing a box
(i,j,k)
will increase the edge-weight by a factor of
q_{i-j}
and adding a box
(i,j,k)
will decrease the edge-weight by a factor of
q_{i-j}
. Let
q^{\omega_{(A,B)}(N)}
be the edge-weight of
\mathbf{D}_{(A,B)}(N)
. It is shown in [
14
] that for
N
large enough, we have
\mathbf{D}_{(\mathrm{III},\mathrm{II}\cup\mathrm{III})}(N)\in\mathbf{D}_{\sigma}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu,\nu}(N))
, which is called the base double-dimer configuration. Denote by
q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu,\nu;N)}
the edge-weight of
\mathbf{D}_{(\mathrm{III},\mathrm{II}\cup\mathrm{III})}(N)
.
Then we have

\displaystyle q^{w_{(A,B)}(N)}\cdot\prod_{l=0}^{n-1}q_{l}^{|A|_{l}+|B|_{l}}=q^{w_{base}(\lambda,\mu,\nu;N)}\cdot\prod_{l=0}^{n-1}q_{l}^{|\mathrm{II}|_{l}+2|\mathrm{III}|_{l}}

if all of the boxes in
A\cup B
correspond to faces of
\mathbf{H}(N)
.
For
N
large enough, one can show as in [
14
] that if
|A|+|B|\leq\frac{N}{2}
and
(A,B)\in\mathcal{AB}
, we have
\mathbf{D}_{(A,B)}(N)\in\mathbf{D}_{\sigma}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu,\nu}(N))
. Then

\displaystyle Z_{\mathcal{AB}}(q_{0}^{-1},q_{1}^{-1},\cdots,q_{n-1}^{-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{(A,B)\in\mathcal{AB}}\prod_{l=0}^{n-1}q_{l}^{-|A|_{l}-|B|_{l}+|\mathrm{II}|_{l}+2|\mathrm{III}|_{l}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(A,B)\in\mathcal{AB}\\
|A|+|B|\leq\frac{N}{2}\end{subarray}}\prod_{l=0}^{n-1}q_{l}^{-|A|_{l}-|B|_{l}+|\mathrm{II}|_{l}+2|\mathrm{III}|_{l}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(A,B)\in\mathcal{AB}\\
|A|+|B|>\frac{N}{2}\end{subarray}}\prod_{l=0}^{n-1}q_{l}^{-|A|_{l}-|B|_{l}+|\mathrm{II}|_{l}+2|\mathrm{III}|_{l}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu,\nu;N)}\right)^{-1}\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(A,B)\in\mathcal{AB}\\
|A|+|B|\leq\frac{N}{2}\end{subarray}}q^{\omega_{(A,B)}(N)}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(A,B)\in\mathcal{AB}\\
|A|+|B|>\frac{N}{2}\end{subarray}}\prod_{l=0}^{n-1}q_{l}^{-|A|_{l}-|B|_{l}+|\mathrm{II}|_{l}+2|\mathrm{III}|_{l}}.

The similar argument as in [
14
] shows that

\displaystyle\left(q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu,\nu;N)}\right)^{-1}\cdot\mathbf{Z}^{DD}_{\sigma}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu,\nu}(N))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu,\nu;N)}\right)^{-1}\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(A,B)\in\mathcal{AB}\\
|A|+|B|\leq\frac{N}{2}\end{subarray}}q^{\omega_{(A,B)}(N)}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(A,B)\in\tau^{-1}(\mathbf{D}_{\sigma}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu,\nu}(N)))\\
|A|+|B|>\frac{N}{2}\end{subarray}}\prod_{l=0}^{n-1}q_{l}^{-|A|_{l}-|B|_{l}+|\mathrm{II}|_{l}+2|\mathrm{III}|_{l}}

where
\tau:\mathcal{AB}_{all}\to\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{H}(N))
maps any AB configuration
(A,B)
to
\mathbf{D}_{(A,B)}(N)
. Here,
\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{H}(N))
is the set of all double-dimer configurations with nodes on
\mathbf{H}(N)
and
\tau^{-1}(\mathbf{D}_{\sigma}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu,\nu}(N)))\subseteq\mathcal{AB}
as shown in [
14
].
Now, by Theorem
5.3
, we have the following orbifold version of [
14
, Theorem 4.4.24].

Theorem 5.6
.

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}^{DD}_{\sigma}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu,\nu}(N))=W^{n}_{\lambda\mu\nu}(q_{0}^{-1},q_{1}^{-1},\cdots,q_{n-1}^{-1})

where

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}^{DD}_{\sigma}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu,\nu}(N)):=\left(q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu,\nu;N)}\right)^{-1}\cdot\mathbf{Z}^{DD}_{\sigma}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu,\nu}(N)).

5.3.
The graphical condensation recurrence for orbifold PT theory

In [
13
], the author has developed an analogue version of [
16
, Theorem 5.1] for the double-dimer case as follows.

Theorem 5.7
.

(See [
13
, Theorem 1.0.2] or [
14
, Theorem 4.5.1]) Let
\mathbf{G}=(\mathbf{V}_{1},\mathbf{V}_{2},\mathbf{E})
be a finite edge-weighted planar bipartitie graph with a set of nodes
\mathbf{N}
. Divide the nodes into three circularly contiguous sets
R
,
G
,
B
such that
|R|
,
|G|
and
|B|
satisfy the triangle inequality and let
\sigma
be the corresponding tripartite pairing. Let
a,b,c,d
be nodes appearing in a cyclic order such that the set
\{a,b,c,d\}
contains at least one node of each RGB color. If
a,c\in\mathbf{V}_{1}
and
b,d\in\mathbf{V}_{2}
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbf{Z}_{\sigma}^{DD}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{N})\mathbf{Z}_{\sigma_{abcd}}^{DD}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{N}-\{a,b,c,d\})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{Z}_{\sigma_{ab}}^{DD}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{N}-\{a,b\})\mathbf{Z}^{DD}_{\sigma_{cd}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{N}-\{c,d\})

\displaystyle+\mathbf{Z}_{\sigma_{ad}}^{DD}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{N}-\{a,d\})\mathbf{Z}^{DD}_{\sigma_{bc}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{N}-\{b,c\})

where
\sigma_{abcd}
is the unique planar pairing on
\mathbf{N}-\{a,b,c,d\}
in which like RGB colors are not paired together, and for
i,j\in\{a,b,c,d\}
,
\sigma_{ij}
is the unique planar pairing on
\mathbf{N}-\{i,j\}
in which like RGB colors are not paired together.

As in [
14
], we choose
\mathbf{G}=\mathbf{H}(N)
,
\mathbf{N}=\mathbf{N}_{\lambda^{rc},\mu^{rc},\nu}(N)
and let

\displaystyle a:=\max S_{1}^{-},\;\;\;b:=\min S_{1}^{+},\;\;\;c=\max S_{2}^{-},\;\;\;d=\min S_{2}^{+}.

Then

\displaystyle(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu,\nu}(N))=(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda^{rc},\mu^{rc},\nu}(N)-\{a,b,c,d\});

\displaystyle(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu^{rc},\nu}(N))=(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda^{rc},\mu^{rc},\nu}(N)-\{a,b\});

\displaystyle(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda^{rc},\mu,\nu}(N))=(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda^{rc},\mu^{rc},\nu}(N)-\{c,d\}).

Now we recall the important description of two double-dimer configurations on
\mathbf{H}(N)
with nodes
\mathbf{N}-\{a,d\}
and
\mathbf{N}-\{b,c\}
in [
14
, Section 4.5] as follows. Since
\mathbf{N}-\{a,d\}=\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu,\nu}(N)\cup\{b,c\}
and
\mathbf{N}-\{b,c\}=\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu,\nu}(N)\cup\{a,d\}
, the pairing
\sigma_{ad}
on
\mathbf{N}-\{a,d\}
has one more blue-green path connecting a blue node
b
in sector 1 and a green node
c
in sector 2 while the pairing
\sigma_{bc}
has one fewer blue-green path but one more red-green path and one more red-blue path than
\sigma_{abcd}
. The double-dimer configuration on
\mathbf{H}(N)
with nodes
\mathbf{N}-\{a,d\}
is obtained from an AB configuration
(A,B)
with respect to
(\lambda^{r},\mu^{c},\nu)
,
but is not equal to
\mathbf{D}_{(A,B)}(N)
. This dimer configuration and the corresponding tilings are shifted directly up by one unit prior to the truncation. We call it the base
up
double-dimer configuration and denote its edge-weight by
q^{\omega^{U}_{base}(\lambda^{r},\mu^{c},\nu;N)}
. Similarly, the double-dimer configuration on
\mathbf{H}(N)
with nodes
\mathbf{N}-\{b,c\}
is derived from an AB configuration with respect to
(\lambda^{c},\mu^{r},\nu)
, and this configuration and its corresponding tilings are shifed directly down by one unit prior to the truncation. And we call it the base
down
double-dimer configuration and denote its edge-weight by
q^{\omega^{D}_{base}(\lambda^{c},\mu^{r},\nu;N)}
. See [
14
, Example 4.5.2] for illustrations.
Let

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda^{rc},\mu^{rc},\nu}(N)):=\left(q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda^{rc},\mu^{rc},\nu;N)}\right)^{-1}\mathbf{Z}_{\sigma}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda^{rc},\mu^{rc},\nu}(N)),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma_{abcd}}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu,\nu}(N)):=\left(q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu,\nu;N)}\right)^{-1}\mathbf{Z}_{\sigma_{abcd}}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu,\nu}(N)),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma_{ab}}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu^{rc},\nu}(N)):=\left(q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu^{rc},\nu;N)}\right)^{-1}\mathbf{Z}_{\sigma_{ab}}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu^{rc},\nu}(N)),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma_{cd}}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda^{rc},\mu,\nu}(N)):=\left(q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda^{rc},\mu,\nu;N)}\right)^{-1}\mathbf{Z}_{\sigma_{cd}}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda^{rc},\mu,\nu}(N)),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma_{ad}}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda^{rc},\mu^{rc},\nu}(N)-\{a,d\}):=\left(q^{\omega^{U}_{base}(\lambda^{r},\mu^{c},\nu;N)}\right)^{-1}\mathbf{Z}_{\sigma_{ad}}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda^{rc},\mu^{rc},\nu}(N)-\{a,d\}),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma_{bc}}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda^{rc},\mu^{rc},\nu}(N)-\{b,c\}):=\left(q^{\omega^{D}_{base}(\lambda^{c},\mu^{r},\nu;N)}\right)^{-1}\mathbf{Z}_{\sigma_{bc}}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda^{rc},\mu^{rc},\nu}(N)-\{b,c\}).

Now, by Theorem
5.7
, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda^{rc},\mu^{rc},\nu}(N))\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma_{abcd}}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu,\nu}(N))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu^{rc},\nu;N)}\cdot q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda^{rc},\mu,\nu;N)}}{q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda^{rc},\mu^{rc},\nu;N)}\cdot q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu,\nu;N)}}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma_{ab}}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu^{rc},\nu}(N))\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma_{cd}}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda^{rc},\mu,\nu}(N))

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{q^{\omega^{U}_{base}(\lambda^{r},\mu^{c},\nu;N)}\cdot q^{\omega^{D}_{base}(\lambda^{c},\mu^{r},\nu;N)}}{q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda^{rc},\mu^{rc},\nu;N)}\cdot q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu,\nu;N)}}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma_{ad}}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda^{rc},\mu^{rc},\nu}(N)-\{a,d\})\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma_{bc}}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda^{rc},\mu^{rc},\nu}(N)-\{b,c\}).

It is calculated in Section 5.4.1 that

\displaystyle\frac{q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu^{rc},\nu;N)}\cdot q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda^{rc},\mu,\nu;N)}}{q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda^{rc},\mu^{rc},\nu;N)}\cdot q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu,\nu;N)}}=1\;\;\;\;\;\;\mbox{and}\;\;\;\;\;\;\frac{q^{\omega^{U}_{base}(\lambda^{r},\mu^{c},\nu;N)}\cdot q^{\omega^{D}_{base}(\lambda^{c},\mu^{r},\nu;N)}}{q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda^{rc},\mu^{rc},\nu;N)}\cdot q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu,\nu;N)}}=q^{-K_{1}(\lambda,\mu,\nu)}

where
q^{-K_{1}(\lambda,\mu,\nu)}
defined in Lemma
5.11
is independent of
N
.
Since

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma_{ad}}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda^{rc},\mu^{rc},\nu}(N)-\{a,d\})=W^{n}_{\lambda^{r}\mu^{c}\nu}(q_{0}^{-1},q_{1}^{-1},\cdots,q_{n-1}^{-1}),

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma_{bc}}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda^{rc},\mu^{rc},\nu}(N)-\{b,c\})=W^{n}_{\lambda^{c}\mu^{r}\nu}(q_{0}^{-1},q_{1}^{-1},\cdots,q_{n-1}^{-1})

by Theorem
5.6
, let
N\to\infty
, we have

\displaystyle W^{n}_{\lambda\mu\nu}(q_{0}^{-1},q_{1}^{-1},\cdots,q_{n-1}^{-1})\cdot W^{n}_{\lambda^{rc}\mu^{rc}\nu}(q_{0}^{-1},q_{1}^{-1},\cdots,q_{n-1}^{-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle W^{n}_{\lambda\mu^{rc}\nu}(q_{0}^{-1},q_{1}^{-1},\cdots,q_{n-1}^{-1})\cdot W^{n}_{\lambda^{rc}\mu\nu}(q_{0}^{-1},q_{1}^{-1},\cdots,q_{n-1}^{-1})

\displaystyle+q^{-K_{1}(\lambda,\mu,\nu)}\cdot W^{n}_{\lambda^{r}\mu^{c}\nu}(q_{0}^{-1},q_{1}^{-1},\cdots,q_{n-1}^{-1})\cdot W^{n}_{\lambda^{c}\mu^{r}\nu}(q_{0}^{-1},q_{1}^{-1},\cdots,q_{n-1}^{-1}).

It is equivalent to

(10)

\displaystyle W^{n}_{\lambda\mu\nu}=W^{n}_{\lambda^{rc}\mu\nu}\cdot W^{n}_{\lambda\mu^{rc}\nu}\cdot\left(W^{n}_{\lambda^{rc}\mu^{rc}\nu}\right)^{-1}+q^{K_{1}(\lambda,\mu,\nu)}\cdot W^{n}_{\lambda^{r}\mu^{c}\nu}\cdot W^{n}_{\lambda^{c}\mu^{r}\nu}\cdot\left(W^{n}_{\lambda^{rc}\mu^{rc}\nu}\right)^{-1}.

which generalizes the one in [
14
, Section 4.5] by Remark
5.12
.

But we still need more recurrences as in the orbifold DT case. First, we take
\mathbf{G}=\mathbf{H}(N)
,
\mathbf{N}=\mathbf{N}_{\lambda^{rc},\mu,\nu^{rc}}(N)
and let

\displaystyle a:=\max S_{1}^{-},\;\;\;b:=\min S_{1}^{+},\;\;\;c=\max S_{3}^{-},\;\;\;d=\min S_{3}^{+}.

Then

\displaystyle(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu,\nu}(N))=(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda^{rc},\mu,\nu^{rc}}(N)-\{a,b,c,d\}),

\displaystyle(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu,\nu^{rc}}(N))=(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda^{rc},\mu,\nu^{rc}}(N)-\{a,b\}),

\displaystyle(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda^{rc},\mu,\nu}(N))=(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda^{rc},\mu,\nu^{rc}}(N)-\{c,d\}).

Since
\mathbf{N}-\{b,c\}=\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu,\nu}(N)\cup\{a,d\}
and
\mathbf{N}-\{a,d\}=\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu,\nu}(N)\cup\{b,c\}
, the pairing
\sigma_{bc}
on
\mathbf{N}-\{b,c\}
has one more red-green path connecting a red node
a
in sector 1 and a green node
d
in sector 3 while the pairing
\sigma_{ad}
has one fewer red-green path but one more blue-red path and one more blue-green path than
\sigma_{abcd}
. The double-dimer configuration on
\mathbf{H}(N)
with nodes
\mathbf{N}-\{a,d\}
is obtained from an AB configuration with respect to
(\lambda^{r},\mu,\nu^{c})
,
but is not equal to
\mathbf{D}_{(A,B)}(N)
. This dimer configuration and the corresponding tilings are shifted in the left-up direction by one unit prior to the truncation. We call it the base
left-up
double-dimer configuration and denote its edge-weight by
q^{\omega^{LU}_{base}(\lambda^{r},\mu,\nu^{c};N)}
. Similarly, the double-dimer configuration on
\mathbf{H}(N)
with nodes
\mathbf{N}-\{b,c\}
is derived from an AB configuration with respect to
(\lambda^{c},\mu,\nu^{r})
, and this configuration and its corresponding tilings are shifed by one unit in the right-down direction prior to the truncation. We call it the base
right-down
double-dimer configuration and denote its edge-weight by
q^{\omega^{RD}_{base}(\lambda^{c},\mu,\nu^{r};N)}
. The figures are omitted and the reader may take an example to draw the pictures (or modify the pictures in [
14
, Example 4.5.2]) for the mentioned shift. Let

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda^{rc},\mu,\nu^{rc}}(N)):=\left(q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda^{rc},\mu,\nu^{rc};N)}\right)^{-1}\mathbf{Z}_{\sigma}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda^{rc},\mu,\nu^{rc}}(N)),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma_{ab}}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu,\nu^{rc}}(N)):=\left(q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu,\nu^{rc};N)}\right)^{-1}\mathbf{Z}_{\sigma_{ab}}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu,\nu^{rc}}(N)),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma_{ad}}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda^{rc},\mu,\nu^{rc}}(N)-\{a,d\}):=\left(q^{\omega^{LU}_{base}(\lambda^{r},\mu,\nu^{c};N)}\right)^{-1}\mathbf{Z}_{\sigma_{ad}}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda^{rc},\mu,\nu^{rc}}(N)-\{a,d\}),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma_{bc}}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda^{rc},\mu,\nu^{rc}}(N)-\{b,c\}):=\left(q^{\omega^{RD}_{base}(\lambda^{c},\mu,\nu^{r};N)}\right)^{-1}\mathbf{Z}_{\sigma_{bc}}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda^{rc},\mu,\nu^{rc}}(N)-\{b,c\}).

By Theorem
5.7
, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda^{rc},\mu,\nu^{rc}}(N))\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma_{abcd}}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu,\nu}(N))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu,\nu^{rc};N)}\cdot q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda^{rc},\mu,\nu;N)}}{q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda^{rc},\mu,\nu^{rc};N)}\cdot q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu,\nu;N)}}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma_{ab}}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu,\nu^{rc}}(N))\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma_{cd}}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda^{rc},\mu,\nu}(N))

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{q^{\omega^{LU}_{base}(\lambda^{r},\mu,\nu^{c};N)}\cdot q^{\omega^{RD}_{base}(\lambda^{c},\mu,\nu^{r};N)}}{q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda^{rc},\mu,\nu^{rc};N)}\cdot q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu,\nu;N)}}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma_{ad}}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda^{rc},\mu,\nu^{rc}}(N)-\{a,d\})\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma_{bc}}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda^{rc},\mu,\nu^{rc}}(N)-\{b,c\}).

It is proved in Section 5.4.2 that

\displaystyle\frac{q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu,\nu^{rc};N)}\cdot q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda^{rc},\mu,\nu;N)}}{q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda^{rc},\mu,\nu^{rc};N)}\cdot q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu,\nu;N)}}=1\;\;\;\;\;\;\mbox{and}\;\;\;\;\;\;\frac{q^{\omega^{LU}_{base}(\lambda^{r},\mu,\nu^{c};N)}\cdot q^{\omega^{RD}_{base}(\lambda^{c},\mu,\nu^{r};N)}}{q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda^{rc},\mu,\nu^{rc};N)}\cdot q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu,\nu;N)}}=q^{-K_{2}(\lambda,\mu,\nu)}

where
q^{-K_{2}(\lambda,\mu,\nu)}
defined in Lemma
5.19
is independent of
N
.
Since

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma_{ad}}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda^{rc},\mu,\nu^{rc}}(N)-\{a,d\})=W^{n}_{\lambda^{r}\mu\nu^{c}}(q_{0}^{-1},q_{1}^{-1},\cdots,q_{n-1}^{-1}),

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma_{bc}}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda^{rc},\mu,\nu^{rc}}(N)-\{b,c\})=W^{n}_{\lambda^{c}\mu\nu^{r}}(q_{0}^{-1},q_{1}^{-1},\cdots,q_{n-1}^{-1})

by Theorem
5.6
, let
N\to\infty
, we have

\displaystyle W^{n}_{\lambda\mu\nu}(q_{0}^{-1},q_{1}^{-1},\cdots,q_{n-1}^{-1})\cdot W^{n}_{\lambda^{rc}\mu\nu^{rc}}(q_{0}^{-1},q_{1}^{-1},\cdots,q_{n-1}^{-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle W^{n}_{\lambda\mu\nu^{rc}}(q_{0}^{-1},q_{1}^{-1},\cdots,q_{n-1}^{-1})\cdot W^{n}_{\lambda^{rc}\mu\nu}(q_{0}^{-1},q_{1}^{-1},\cdots,q_{n-1}^{-1})

\displaystyle+q^{-K_{2}(\lambda,\mu,\nu)}\cdot W^{n}_{\lambda^{r}\mu\nu^{c}}(q_{0}^{-1},q_{1}^{-1},\cdots,q_{n-1}^{-1})\cdot W^{n}_{\lambda^{c}\mu\nu^{r}}(q_{0}^{-1},q_{1}^{-1},\cdots,q_{n-1}^{-1})

which is equivalent to

(11)

\displaystyle W^{n}_{\lambda\mu\nu}=W^{n}_{\lambda^{rc}\mu\nu}\cdot W^{n}_{\lambda\mu\nu^{rc}}\cdot\left(W^{n}_{\lambda^{rc}\mu\nu^{rc}}\right)^{-1}+q^{K_{2}(\lambda,\mu,\nu)}\cdot W^{n}_{\lambda^{r}\mu\nu^{c}}\cdot W^{n}_{\lambda^{c}\mu\nu^{r}}\left(W^{n}_{\lambda^{rc}\mu\nu^{rc}}\right)^{-1}.

As in DT case, one can derive another graphical condensation recurrence from the symmetry of
W^{n}_{\lambda\mu\nu}
and Equation (
11
) as follows

\displaystyle\overline{W}^{n}_{\mu^{\prime}\lambda^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}\cdot\overline{W}^{n}_{\mu^{\prime}(\lambda^{\prime})^{rc}(\nu^{\prime})^{rc}}=\overline{W}^{n}_{\mu^{\prime}\lambda^{\prime}(\nu^{\prime})^{rc}}\cdot\overline{W}^{n}_{\mu^{\prime}(\lambda^{\prime})^{rc}\nu^{\prime}}+\overline{q^{K_{3}(\mu^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime},\nu^{\prime})}}\cdot\overline{W}^{n}_{\mu^{\prime}(\lambda^{\prime})^{c}(\nu^{\prime})^{r}}\cdot\overline{W}^{n}_{\mu^{\prime}(\lambda^{\prime})^{r}(\nu^{\prime})^{c}}

which is equivalent to Equation (
12
). Similarly, the relations among Equations (
10
), (
11
), (
12
) are similar as the orbifold DT case. Independently, the graphical condensation recurrence (
12
) can be obtained by setting

\mathbf{G}=\mathbf{H}(N)
,
\mathbf{N}=\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu^{rc},\nu^{rc}}(N)
and

\displaystyle a:=\max S_{2}^{-},\;\;\;b:=\min S_{2}^{+},\;\;\;c=\max S_{3}^{-},\;\;\;d=\min S_{3}^{+}.

Then

\displaystyle(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu,\nu}(N))=(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu^{rc},\nu^{rc}}(N)-\{a,b,c,d\});

\displaystyle(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu,\nu^{rc}}(N))=(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu^{rc},\nu^{rc}}(N)-\{a,b\});

\displaystyle(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu^{rc},\nu}(N))=(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu^{rc},\nu^{rc}}(N)-\{c,d\}).

Since
\mathbf{N}-\{a,d\}=\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu,\nu}(N)\cup\{b,c\}
and
\mathbf{N}-\{b,c\}=\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu,\nu}(N)\cup\{a,d\}
, the pairing
\sigma_{ad}
on
\mathbf{N}-\{a,d\}
has one more red-blue path connecting a red node
b
in sector 2 and a blue node
c
in sector 3 while the pairing
\sigma_{bc}
has one fewer red-blue path but one more green-red path and one more green-blue path than
\sigma_{abcd}
. The double-dimer configuration on
\mathbf{H}(N)
with nodes
\mathbf{N}-\{a,d\}
is obtained from an AB configuration
(A,B)
with respect to
(\lambda,\mu^{r},\nu^{c})
,
but is not equal to
\mathbf{D}_{(A,B)}(N)
. This dimer configuration and the corresponding tilings are shifted in the left-down direction by one unit prior to truncation. We call it the base
left-down
double-dimer configuration and denote its edge-weight by
q^{\omega^{LD}_{base}(\lambda,\mu^{r},\nu^{c};N)}
. Similarly, the double-dimer configuration on
\mathbf{H}(N)
with nodes
\mathbf{N}-\{b,c\}
is derived from an AB configuration with respect to
(\lambda,\mu^{c},\nu^{r})
, and this configuration and its corresponding tilings are shifed by one unit in the right-up direction prior to truncation. We call it the base
right-up
double-dimer configuration and denote its edge-weight by
q^{\omega^{RU}_{base}(\lambda,\mu^{c},\nu^{r};N)}
. Let

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu^{rc},\nu^{rc}}(N)):=\left(q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu^{rc},\nu^{rc};N)}\right)^{-1}\mathbf{Z}_{\sigma}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu^{rc},\nu^{rc}}(N)),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma_{cd}}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu^{rc},\nu}(N)):=\left(q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu^{rc},\nu;N)}\right)^{-1}\mathbf{Z}_{\sigma_{cd}}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu^{rc},\nu}(N)),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma_{ad}}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu^{rc},\nu^{rc}}(N)-\{a,d\}):=\left(q^{\omega^{LD}_{base}(\lambda,\mu^{r},\nu^{c};N)}\right)^{-1}\mathbf{Z}_{\sigma_{ad}}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu^{rc},\nu^{rc}}(N)-\{a,d\}),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma_{bc}}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu^{rc},\nu^{rc}}(N)-\{b,c\}):=\left(q^{\omega^{RU}_{base}(\lambda,\mu^{c},\nu^{r};N)}\right)^{-1}\mathbf{Z}_{\sigma_{bc}}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu^{rc},\nu^{rc}}(N)-\{b,c\}).

By Theorem
5.7
, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu^{rc},\nu^{rc}}(N))\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma_{abcd}}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu,\nu}(N))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu,\nu^{rc};N)}\cdot q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu^{rc},\nu;N)}}{q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu^{rc},\nu^{rc};N)}\cdot q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu,\nu;N)}}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma_{ab}}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu,\nu^{rc}}(N))\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma_{cd}}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu^{rc},\nu}(N))

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{q^{\omega^{LD}_{base}(\lambda,\mu^{r},\nu^{c};N)}\cdot q^{\omega^{RU}_{base}(\lambda,\mu^{c},\nu^{r};N)}}{q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu^{rc},\nu^{rc};N)}\cdot q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu,\nu;N)}}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma_{ad}}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu^{rc},\nu^{rc}}(N)-\{a,d\})\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma_{bc}}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu^{rc},\nu^{rc}}(N)-\{b,c\})

It is proved in Section 5.4.3 that

\displaystyle\frac{q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu,\nu^{rc};N)}\cdot q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu^{rc},\nu;N)}}{q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu^{rc},\nu^{rc};N)}\cdot q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu,\nu;N)}}=1\;\;\;\;\;\;\mbox{and}\;\;\;\;\;\;\frac{q^{\omega^{LD}_{base}(\lambda,\mu^{r},\nu^{c};N)}\cdot q^{\omega^{RU}_{base}(\lambda,\mu^{c},\nu^{r};N)}}{q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu^{rc},\nu^{rc};N)}\cdot q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu,\nu;N)}}=q^{-K_{3}(\lambda,\mu,\nu)}

where
q^{-K_{3}(\lambda,\mu,\nu)}
defined in Lemma
5.26
is independent of
N
.
Since

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma_{ad}}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu^{rc},\nu^{rc}}(N)-\{a,d\})=W^{n}_{\lambda\mu^{r}\nu^{c}}(q_{0}^{-1},q_{1}^{-1},\cdots,q_{n-1}^{-1}),

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma_{bc}}^{DD}(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu^{rc},\nu^{rc}}(N)-\{b,c\})=W^{n}_{\lambda\mu^{c}\nu^{r}}(q_{0}^{-1},q_{1}^{-1},\cdots,q_{n-1}^{-1})

by Theorem
5.6
, let
N\to\infty
, we have

\displaystyle W^{n}_{\lambda\mu\nu}(q_{0}^{-1},q_{1}^{-1},\cdots,q_{n-1}^{-1})\cdot W^{n}_{\lambda\mu^{rc}\nu^{rc}}(q_{0}^{-1},q_{1}^{-1},\cdots,q_{n-1}^{-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle W^{n}_{\lambda\mu\nu^{rc}}(q_{0}^{-1},q_{1}^{-1},\cdots,q_{n-1}^{-1})\cdot W^{n}_{\lambda\mu^{rc}\nu}(q_{0}^{-1},q_{1}^{-1},\cdots,q_{n-1}^{-1})

\displaystyle+q^{-K_{3}(\lambda,\mu,\nu)}\cdot W^{n}_{\lambda\mu^{r}\nu^{c}}(q_{0}^{-1},q_{1}^{-1},\cdots,q_{n-1}^{-1})\cdot W^{n}_{\lambda\mu^{c}\nu^{r}}(q_{0}^{-1},q_{1}^{-1},\cdots,q_{n-1}^{-1}).

Now, we have

(12)

\displaystyle W^{n}_{\lambda\mu\nu}=W^{n}_{\lambda\mu^{rc}\nu}\cdot W^{n}_{\lambda\mu\nu^{rc}}\cdot\left(W^{n}_{\lambda\mu^{rc}\nu^{rc}}\right)^{-1}+q^{K_{3}(\lambda,\mu,\nu)}\cdot W^{n}_{\lambda\mu^{r}\nu^{c}}\cdot W^{n}_{\lambda\mu^{c}\nu^{r}}\cdot\left(W^{n}_{\lambda\mu^{rc}\nu^{rc}}\right)^{-1}.

5.4.
Weights for orbifold PT theory

For simplifications, we introduce the following notations

\displaystyle q^{\vartheta_{1}(\eta,m,k,l)}:=\prod_{i=1}^{m-\ell(\eta)-l}\prod_{j=1}^{i}q_{-m+i-j+1}^{i+k},

\displaystyle q^{\vartheta_{2}(\eta,m,k)}:=\prod_{i:1\leq i\leq\eta_{i}}\prod_{j=1}^{m-\eta_{i}}q_{-i-j+k+1}^{m+\eta_{i}-i-k}\cdot\prod_{i:\eta_{i}<i\leq\ell(\eta)}\prod_{j=1}^{m-i}q_{-i-j+k+1}^{m+\eta_{i}-i-k},

\displaystyle q^{\vartheta_{3}(\eta,m,k,l)}:=\prod_{i=1}^{m-\ell(\eta)-1}\prod_{j=\ell(\eta)+1}^{m-i}q_{i+j-l}^{m+i-k},

\displaystyle q^{\vartheta_{4}(\eta,m,k)}:=\prod_{i:1\leq i\leq\eta_{i}}\prod_{j=1}^{m-\eta_{i}-k}q_{i+j+k-1}^{m+\eta_{i}+j+k-1}\cdot\prod_{i:\eta_{i}<i\leq\ell(\eta)}\prod_{j=1}^{m-i-k}q_{i+j+k-1}^{m+\eta_{i}+j+k-1},

\displaystyle q^{\vartheta_{5}(\eta,m,k)}:=\left\{\begin{aligned} &1,\;\;\;\mbox{if $\eta=\emptyset$},\\
&\prod_{i:1\leq i\leq\eta_{i}+1}\prod_{j=1}^{m-\eta_{i}-1}q_{-i-j+k+1}^{m+\eta_{i}-i-k+1}\cdot\prod_{i:\eta_{i}+1<i\leq\ell(\eta)}\prod_{j=1}^{m-i}q_{-i-j+k+1}^{m+\eta_{i}-i-k+1},\;\;\;\mbox{if $\eta\neq\emptyset$},\end{aligned}\right.

\displaystyle q^{\vartheta_{6}(\eta,m)}:=\left\{\begin{aligned} &1,\;\;\;\mbox{if $\eta=\emptyset$},\\
&\prod_{i:1\leq i\leq\eta_{i}+1}\prod_{j=1}^{m-\eta_{i}-1}q_{i+j-1}^{m+\eta_{i}+j}\cdot\prod_{i:\eta_{i}+1<i\leq\ell(\eta)}\prod_{j=1}^{m-i}q_{i+j-1}^{m+\eta_{i}+j},\;\;\;\mbox{if $\eta\neq\emptyset$},\end{aligned}\right.

\displaystyle q^{\vartheta_{7}(\eta,m,k)}:=\prod_{i:1\leq i<\eta_{i}-1}\prod_{j=1}^{m-\eta_{i}+k}q_{-i-j+k}^{m+\eta_{i}-i-1}\cdot\prod_{i:\eta_{i}-1\leq i\leq\ell(\eta)}\prod_{j=1}^{m-i+k-1}q_{-i-j+k}^{m+\eta_{i}-i-1},

\displaystyle q^{\vartheta_{8}(\eta,m,k,l)}:=\prod_{i:1\leq i<\eta_{i}-k}\prod_{j=1}^{m-\eta_{i}}q_{i+j+k-l-1}^{m+\eta_{i}+j-2l-1}\cdot\prod_{i:\eta_{i}-k\leq i\leq\ell(\eta)}\prod_{j=1}^{m-i-1}q_{i+j+k-l-1}^{m+\eta_{i}+j-2l-1},

where
m,l,k
are all integers and
\eta
is a partition.

5.4.1.
Weights for the graphical condensation recurrence with
(\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda^{rc},\mu^{rc},\nu}(N))

We will compute the edge-weights of several double-dimer configurations as in [
14
, Section 5.3] with the given weighted rule in Definition
4.3
.
Since the base double-dimer configuration
\mathbf{D}_{(\mathrm{III},\mathrm{II}\cup\mathrm{III})}(N)
has the edge-weight
q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu,\nu;N)}
, which is obtained from the weights of all horizontal dimers of
\mathbf{D}_{A}(N)
and the weights of all horizontal dimers of
\mathbf{D}_{B}(N)
where
(A,B)=(\mathrm{III},\mathrm{II}\cup\mathrm{III})
. For the horizontal dimers of
\mathbf{D}_{A}(N)
in sector 1, one can divide them into
\ell(\lambda^{\prime})+1
groups with the group
i
along the
i
-th part of
\lambda^{\prime}
if
1\leq i\leq\ell(\lambda^{\prime})
and the others as a group if
i>\ell(\lambda^{\prime})
. For
i>\ell(\lambda^{\prime})
, the product of weights of all the edges in this group is
\prod\limits_{i=1}^{N-\ell(\lambda^{\prime})-1}\prod\limits_{j=1}^{i}q_{-N+i-j+1}^{i}
. If
\lambda^{\prime}_{i}\geq i
, there are still
(N-\lambda^{\prime}_{i})
horizontal dimers in
\mathbf{H}(N)
with weights:
q_{-i}^{N+\lambda^{\prime}_{i}-i},\cdots,q_{-i-N+\lambda^{\prime}_{i}+1}^{N+\lambda^{\prime}_{i}-i}
. If
\lambda^{\prime}_{i}<i
, there are still
(N-i)
horizontal dimers in
\mathbf{H}(N)
with weights:
q_{-i}^{N+\lambda^{\prime}_{i}-i},\cdots,q_{1-N}^{N+\lambda^{\prime}_{i}-i}
. Similarly, one can divide all horizontal dimers of
\mathbf{D}_{A}(N)
in sector 2 into
\ell(\mu)+1
groups with the group
i
along the
i
-th part of
\mu
if
1\leq i\leq\ell(\mu)
and the others as a group if
i>\ell(\mu)
. For
i>\ell(\mu)
, the product of weights of all the edges in this group
is
\prod\limits_{i=1}^{N-\ell(\mu)-1}\prod\limits_{j=\ell(\mu)+1}^{N-i}q_{i+j-1}^{N+i-1}
. If
\mu_{i}\geq i
, there are still
(N-\mu_{i})
horizontal dimers in
\mathbf{H}(N)
with weights:
q_{i}^{N+\mu_{i}},q_{i+1}^{N+\mu_{i}+1},\cdots,q_{i+N-\mu_{i}-1}^{2N-1}
. If
\mu_{i}<i
, there are still
(N-i)
horizontal dimers in
\mathbf{H}(N)
with weights:
q_{i}^{N+\mu_{i}},q_{i+1}^{N+\mu_{i}+1},\cdots,q_{N-1}^{2N+\mu_{i}-i-1}
. The total weight from the horizontal dimers of
\mathbf{D}_{A}(N)
in sector 3 is
\prod\limits_{i=1}^{\ell(\nu)}\prod\limits_{j=0}^{\nu_{i}-1}q_{j-i+1}^{N-i}
. Since the horizontal dimers of
\mathbf{D}_{B}(N)
appear only in sector 3, the total weight from
\mathbf{D}_{B}(N)
is
\prod\limits_{i=0}^{N-1}\prod\limits_{j=0}^{N-1}q_{j-i}^{N-i-1}
.
Therefore, we have

Lemma 5.8
.

The edge-weight of the base double-dimer configuration is

\displaystyle q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu,\nu;N)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{N-\ell(\lambda^{\prime})-1}\prod_{j=1}^{i}q_{-N+i-j+1}^{i}\cdot\prod_{i:1\leq i\leq\lambda^{\prime}_{i}}\prod_{j=1}^{N-\lambda^{\prime}_{i}}q_{-i-j+1}^{N+\lambda^{\prime}_{i}-i}\cdot\prod_{i:\lambda^{\prime}_{i}<i\leq\ell(\lambda^{\prime})}\prod_{j=1}^{N-i}q_{-i-j+1}^{N+\lambda^{\prime}_{i}-i}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=1}^{N-\ell(\mu)-1}\prod_{j=\ell(\mu)+1}^{N-i}q_{i+j-1}^{N+i-1}\cdot\prod_{i:1\leq i\leq\mu_{i}}\prod_{j=1}^{N-\mu_{i}}q_{i+j-1}^{N+\mu_{i}+j-1}\cdot\prod_{i:\mu_{i}<i\leq\ell(\mu)}\prod_{j=1}^{N-i}q_{i+j-1}^{N+\mu_{i}+j-1}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=1}^{\ell(\nu)}\prod_{j=0}^{\nu_{i}-1}q_{j-i+1}^{N-i}\cdot\prod_{i=0}^{N-1}\prod_{j=0}^{N-1}q_{j-i}^{N-i-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q^{\vartheta_{1}(\lambda^{\prime},N,0,1)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{2}(\lambda^{\prime},N,0)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{3}(\mu,N,1,1)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{4}(\mu,N,0)}\cdot\left(q^{\varpi_{3}(\nu,N,1)}\right)^{-1}\cdot q^{\varpi_{1}(N-1,0,0)}.

Similarly, we have

Lemma 5.9
.

The edge-weight of the base
up
double-dimer configuration is

\displaystyle q^{\omega_{base}^{U}(\lambda^{r},\mu^{c},\nu;n)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{N-\ell((\lambda^{r})^{\prime})-1}\prod_{j=1}^{i}q_{-N+i-j+1}^{i+1}\cdot\prod_{i:1\leq i\leq(\lambda^{r})^{\prime}_{i}+1}\prod_{j=1}^{N-(\lambda^{r})^{\prime}_{i}-1}q_{-i-j+1}^{N+(\lambda^{r})^{\prime}_{i}-i+1}\cdot\prod_{i:(\lambda^{r})^{\prime}_{i}+1<i\leq\ell((\lambda^{r})^{\prime})}\prod_{j=1}^{N-i}q_{-i-j+1}^{N+(\lambda^{r})^{\prime}_{i}-i+1}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=1}^{N-\ell(\mu^{c})-1}\prod_{j=\ell(\mu^{c})+1}^{N-i}q_{i+j-1}^{N+i}\cdot\prod_{i:1\leq i\leq\mu^{c}_{i}+1}\prod_{j=1}^{N-\mu^{c}_{i}-1}q_{i+j-1}^{N+\mu^{c}_{i}+j}\cdot\prod_{i:\mu^{c}_{i}+1<i\leq\ell(\mu^{c})}\prod_{j=1}^{N-i}q_{i+j-1}^{N+\mu^{c}_{i}+j}

\displaystyle\times q_{N}^{-N}\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{\ell(\nu)}\prod_{j=0}^{\nu_{i}-1}q_{j-i+1}^{N-i+1}\cdot\prod_{i=0}^{N}\prod_{j=0}^{N}q_{j-i}^{N-i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q^{\vartheta_{1}((\lambda^{\prime})^{c},N,1,1)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{5}((\lambda^{\prime})^{c},N,0)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{3}(\mu^{c},N,0,1)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{6}(\mu^{c},N)}\cdot\left(q^{\varpi_{3}(\nu,N+1,1)}\right)^{-1}\cdot q^{\varpi_{1}(N,0,0)}\cdot q_{N}^{-N}.

Lemma 5.10
.

The edge-weight of the base
down
double-dimer configuration is

\displaystyle q^{\omega_{base}^{D}(\lambda^{c},\mu^{r},\nu;n)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{N-\ell((\lambda^{c})^{\prime})-1}\prod_{j=1}^{i}q_{-N+i-j+1}^{i-1}\cdot\prod_{i:1\leq i<(\lambda^{c})^{\prime}_{i}-1}\prod_{j=1}^{N-(\lambda^{c})^{\prime}_{i}+1}q_{-i-j+1}^{N+(\lambda^{c})^{\prime}_{i}-i-1}\cdot\prod_{i:(\lambda^{c})^{\prime}_{i}-1\leq i\leq\ell((\lambda^{c})^{\prime})}\prod_{j=1}^{N-i}q_{-i-j+1}^{N+(\lambda^{c})^{\prime}_{i}-i-1}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=1}^{N-\ell(\mu^{r})-1}\prod_{j=\ell(\mu^{r})+1}^{N-i}q_{i+j-1}^{N+i-2}\cdot\prod_{i:1\leq i<\mu^{r}_{i}-1}\prod_{j=1}^{N-\mu^{r}_{i}+1}q_{i+j-1}^{N+\mu^{r}_{i}+j-2}\cdot\prod_{i:\mu^{r}_{i}-1\leq i\leq\ell(\mu^{r})}\prod_{j=1}^{N-i}q_{i+j-1}^{N+\mu^{r}_{i}+j-2}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=1}^{\ell(\nu)}\prod_{j=0}^{\nu_{i}-1}q_{j-i+1}^{N-i-1}\cdot\prod_{i=0}^{N-2}\prod_{j=0}^{N-2}q_{j-i}^{N-i-2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q^{\vartheta_{1}((\lambda^{\prime})^{r},N,-1,1)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{7}((\lambda^{\prime})^{r},N,1)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{3}(\mu^{r},N,2,1)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{8}(\mu^{r},N+1,1,1)}\cdot\left(q^{\varpi_{3}(\nu,N-1,1)}\right)^{-1}\cdot q^{\varpi_{1}(N-2,0,0)}.

Now it follows from Lemma
5.8
that

\displaystyle\frac{q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu^{rc},\nu;N)}\cdot q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda^{rc},\mu,\nu;N)}}{q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda^{rc},\mu^{rc},\nu;N)}\cdot q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu,\nu;N)}}=1.

And we still need the following lemmas to derive the graphical condensation recurrence (
10
).

Lemma 5.11
.

For any partition
\lambda,\mu\neq\emptyset
and
\nu
,

\displaystyle\dfrac{q^{\omega_{base}^{U}(\lambda^{r},\mu^{c},\nu;N)}\cdot q^{\omega_{base}^{D}(\lambda^{c},\mu^{r},\nu;N)}}{q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu,\nu;N)}\cdot q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda^{rc},\mu^{rc},\nu;N)}}=q^{-K_{1}(\lambda,\mu,\nu)}

where
q^{-K_{1}(\lambda,\mu,\nu)}=\left(q^{K_{1}(\lambda,\mu,\nu)}\right)^{-1}
is independent of
N
.

Proof.

It follows from Lemmas
5.8
,
5.9
,
5.10
,
5.13
,
5.14
,

5.15
,
5.16
,
4.21
,
4.19
.
∎

Remark 5.12
.

When
n=1
,

q^{-K_{1}(\lambda,\mu,\nu)}
is equal to
q^{K}
in [
14
, Section 4.5] as in Remark
4.18
.

Lemma 5.13
.

For any partition
\eta\neq\emptyset
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{q^{\vartheta_{1}(\eta^{c},N,1,1)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{1}(\eta^{r},N,-1,1)}}{q^{\vartheta_{1}(\eta,N,0,1)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{1}(\eta^{rc},N,0,1)}}=\left\{\begin{aligned} &q_{-\ell(\eta)}^{N-\ell(\eta)-1}\cdot\prod\limits_{i=1-N}^{-\ell(\eta)-1}q_{i}^{-1},\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\mbox{if $d(\eta)>1$};\\
&\prod\limits_{i=1-N}^{-\ell(\eta)}q_{i}^{-1}\cdot\prod\limits_{i=1-\ell(\eta)}^{-1}q_{i}^{-N-i},\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\mbox{if $d(\eta)=1$ and $\eta_{1}>1$};\\
&\prod\limits_{i=1-N}^{-\ell(\eta)}q_{i}^{N-1}\cdot\prod\limits_{i=1-\ell(\eta)}^{-1}q_{i}^{-i},\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\mbox{if $d(\eta)=1$ and $\eta_{1}=1$}.\end{aligned}\right.

Proof.

If
d(\eta)>1
, by Lemma
4.9
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{q^{\vartheta_{1}(\eta^{c},N,1,1)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{1}(\eta^{r},N,-1,1)}}{q^{\vartheta_{1}(\eta,N,0,1)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{1}(\eta^{rc},N,0,1)}}=\frac{\prod\limits_{i=1}^{N-\ell(\eta)-2}\prod\limits_{j=1}^{i}q_{-N+i-j+1}^{i+1}\cdot\prod\limits_{i=1}^{N-\ell(\eta)}\prod\limits_{j=1}^{i}q_{-N+i-j+1}^{i-1}}{\prod\limits_{i=1}^{N-\ell(\eta)-1}\prod\limits_{j=1}^{i}q_{-N+i-j+1}^{2i}}=\frac{q_{-\ell(\eta)}^{N-\ell(\eta)-1}}{\prod\limits_{j=1}^{N-\ell(\eta)-1}q_{-\ell(\eta)-j}}.

If
d(\eta)=1
and
\eta_{1}>1
, then by Lemma
4.9
and Remark
4.11

\displaystyle\frac{q^{\vartheta_{1}(\eta^{c},N,1,1)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{1}(\eta^{r},N,-1,1)}}{q^{\vartheta_{1}(\eta,N,0,1)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{1}(\eta^{rc},N,0,1)}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\prod\limits_{i=N-\ell(\eta)}^{N-1}\prod\limits_{j=1}^{i}q_{-N+i-j+1}^{i}\cdot\prod\limits_{i=1}^{N-1}\prod\limits_{j=1}^{i}q_{-N+i-j+1}^{i+1}\cdot\prod\limits_{i=1}^{N-1}\prod\limits_{j=1}^{i}q_{-N+i-j+1}^{i-1}}{\prod\limits_{j=1}^{N-1}q_{-j}^{N}\cdot\prod\limits_{i=N-\ell(\eta)+1}^{N-1}\prod\limits_{j=1}^{i}q_{-N+i-j+1}^{i-1}\cdot\prod\limits_{i=1}^{N-1}\prod\limits_{j=1}^{i}q_{-N+i-j+1}^{i}\cdot\prod\limits_{i=1}^{N-1}\prod\limits_{j=1}^{i}q_{-N+i-j+1}^{i}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\prod\limits_{j=1-N}^{-\ell(\eta)}q_{j}\cdot\prod\limits_{j=1-\ell(\eta)}^{-1}q_{j}^{N+j}}.

If
d(\eta)=1
and
\eta_{1}=1
, then by Lemma
4.9
and Remark
4.11

\displaystyle\frac{q^{\vartheta_{1}(\eta^{c},N,1,1)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{1}(\eta^{r},N,-1,1)}}{q^{\vartheta_{1}(\eta,N,0,1)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{1}(\eta^{rc},N,0,1)}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\prod\limits_{i=N-\ell(\eta)}^{N-1}\prod\limits_{j=1}^{i}q_{-N+i-j+1}^{i}\cdot\prod\limits_{i=1}^{N-1}\prod\limits_{j=1}^{i}q_{-N+i-j+1}^{i+1}\cdot\prod\limits_{i=1}^{N-1}\prod\limits_{j=1}^{i}q_{-N+i-j+1}^{i-1}}{\prod\limits_{i=N-\ell(\eta)+1}^{N-1}\prod\limits_{j=1}^{i}q_{-N+i-j+1}^{i-1}\cdot\prod\limits_{i=1}^{N-1}\prod\limits_{j=1}^{i}q_{-N+i-j+1}^{i}\cdot\prod\limits_{i=1}^{N-1}\prod\limits_{j=1}^{i}q_{-N+i-j+1}^{i}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\prod\limits_{j=1-N}^{-\ell(\eta)}q_{j}^{N-1}\cdot\prod\limits_{j=1-\ell(\eta)}^{-1}q_{j}^{-j}.

∎

Lemma 5.14
.

For any partition
\eta\neq\emptyset
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{q^{\vartheta_{5}(\eta^{c},N,0)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{7}(\eta^{r},N,1)}}{q^{\vartheta_{2}(\eta,N,0)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{2}(\eta^{rc},N,0)}}=\left\{\begin{aligned} &q_{-\widetilde{d}(\eta)}^{-d(\eta)}\cdot q_{-\ell(\eta)}^{-N+\ell(\eta)}\cdot\prod\limits_{i=\widetilde{d}(\eta)+1}^{\ell(\eta)-1}q_{-i}^{-1}\cdot\prod\limits_{i=\widetilde{d}(\eta)+1}^{\ell(\eta)}\left(\frac{q_{-i+1}}{q_{-i}}\right)^{\eta_{i}},\;\;\;\;\;\mbox{if $d(\eta)>1$};\\
&\prod\limits_{i=1}^{\ell(\eta)-1}q_{-i}^{N-i},\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\mbox{if $d(\eta)=1$ and $\eta_{1}>1$};\\
&\prod\limits_{i=1}^{\ell(\eta)-1}q_{-i}^{-i}\cdot\prod\limits_{i=\ell(\eta)}^{N-1}q_{-i}^{-N},\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\mbox{if $d(\eta)=1$ and $\eta_{1}=1$}.\end{aligned}\right.

Proof.

If
d(\eta)>1
, then
\eta^{c}\neq\emptyset
by Remark
4.11
. Then by Lemmas
4.7
,
4.8
,
4.13
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{q^{\vartheta_{5}(\eta^{c},N,0)}}{q^{\vartheta_{2}(\eta,N,0)}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\prod\limits_{i=1}^{d(\eta)}\prod\limits_{j=1}^{N-\eta_{i}}q_{-i-j+1}^{N+\eta_{i}-i}\cdot\prod\limits_{i=d(\eta)+1}^{\widetilde{d}(\eta)}\prod\limits_{j=1}^{N-i}q_{-i-j+1}^{N+d(\eta)-i}\cdot\prod\limits_{j=1}^{N-\widetilde{d}(\eta)-1}q_{-\widetilde{d}(\eta)-j}^{N+d(\eta)-\widetilde{d}(\eta)-1}\cdot\prod\limits_{i=\widetilde{d}(\eta)+2}^{\ell(\eta)+1}\prod\limits_{j=1}^{N-i}q_{-i-j+1}^{N+\eta_{i-1}-i+1}}{\prod\limits_{i=1}^{d(\eta)}\prod\limits_{j=1}^{N-\eta_{i}}q_{-i-j+1}^{N+\eta_{i}-i}\cdot\prod\limits_{i=d(\eta)+1}^{\widetilde{d}(\eta)}\prod\limits_{j=1}^{N-i}q_{-i-j+1}^{N+d(\eta)-i}\cdot\prod\limits_{i=\widetilde{d}(\eta)+1}^{\ell(\eta)}\prod\limits_{j=1}^{N-i}q_{-i-j+1}^{N+\eta_{i}-i}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\prod\limits_{j=1}^{N-\widetilde{d}(\eta)-1}q_{-\widetilde{d}(\eta)-j}^{N+d(\eta)-\widetilde{d}(\eta)-1}}{\prod\limits_{i=\widetilde{d}(\eta)+1}^{\ell(\eta)}q_{-i}^{N+\eta_{i}-i}}

and

\displaystyle\frac{q^{\vartheta_{7}(\eta^{r},N,1)}}{q^{\vartheta_{2}(\eta^{rc},N,0)}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\prod\limits_{i=1}^{d(\eta)-1}\prod\limits_{j=1}^{N-\eta_{i}}q_{-i-j+1}^{N+\eta_{i}-i}\cdot\prod\limits_{i=d(\eta)}^{\ell(\eta)-1}\prod\limits_{j=1}^{N-i}q_{-i-j+1}^{N+\eta_{i+1}-i-1}}{\prod\limits_{i=1}^{d(\eta)-1}\prod\limits_{j=1}^{N-\eta_{i}}q_{-i-j+1}^{N+\eta_{i}-i}\cdot\prod\limits_{i=d(\eta)}^{\widetilde{d}(\eta)}\prod\limits_{j=1}^{N-i}q_{-i-j+1}^{N+d(\eta)-i-1}\cdot\prod\limits_{i=\widetilde{d}(\eta)+1}^{\ell(\eta)}\prod\limits_{j=1}^{N-i}q_{-i-j+1}^{N+\eta_{i}-i}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\prod\limits_{i=d(\eta)+1}^{\widetilde{d}(\eta)}q_{-i+1}^{N+d(\eta)-i}\cdot\prod\limits_{i=d(\eta)+1}^{\widetilde{d}(\eta)}\prod\limits_{j=1}^{N-i}q_{-i-j+1}\cdot\prod\limits_{i=\widetilde{d}(\eta)+1}^{\ell(\eta)}q_{-i+1}^{N+\eta_{i}-i}}{\prod\limits_{j=1}^{N-d(\eta)}q_{-d(\eta)-j+1}^{N-1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\prod\limits_{j=0}^{N-\widetilde{d}(\eta)-1}q_{-\widetilde{d}(\eta)-j}^{-N-d(\eta)+\widetilde{d}(\eta)+1}\cdot\prod\limits_{i=\widetilde{d}(\eta)+1}^{\ell(\eta)}q_{-i+1}^{N+\eta_{i}-i}.

Then

\displaystyle\frac{q^{\vartheta_{5}(\eta^{c},N,0)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{7}(\eta^{r},N,1)}}{q^{\vartheta_{2}(\eta,N,0)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{2}(\eta^{rc},N,0)}}=\frac{q_{-\widetilde{d}(\eta)}^{-d(\eta)}\cdot q_{-\ell(\eta)}^{-N+\ell(\eta)}}{\prod\limits_{i=\widetilde{d}(\eta)+1}^{\ell(\eta)-1}q_{-i}}\prod\limits_{i=\widetilde{d}(\eta)+1}^{\ell(\eta)}\left(\frac{q_{-i+1}}{q_{-i}}\right)^{\eta_{i}}.

If
d(\eta)=1
and
\eta_{1}>1
, then
\eta^{c}\neq\emptyset
by Remark
4.11
. Now we have

\displaystyle\frac{q^{\vartheta_{5}(\eta^{c},N,0)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{7}(\eta^{r},N,1)}}{q^{\vartheta_{2}(\eta,N,0)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{2}(\eta^{rc},N,0)}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\prod\limits_{j=1}^{N-\eta_{1}}q_{-j}^{N+\eta_{1}-1}\cdot\prod\limits_{i=2}^{\ell(\eta)}\prod\limits_{j=0}^{N-i}q_{-i-j+1}^{N+1-i}}{\prod\limits_{j=1}^{N-\eta_{1}}q_{-j}^{N+\eta_{1}-1}\cdot\prod\limits_{i=2}^{\ell(\eta)}\prod\limits_{j=1}^{N-i}q_{-i-j+1}^{N+1-i}}=\prod\limits_{i=1}^{\ell(\eta)-1}q_{-i}^{N-i}.

If
d(\eta)=1
and
\eta_{1}=1
, then
\eta^{c}=\emptyset
by Remark
4.11
. Then we have

\displaystyle\frac{q^{\vartheta_{5}(\eta^{c},N,0)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{7}(\eta^{r},N,1)}}{q^{\vartheta_{2}(\eta,N,0)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{2}(\eta^{rc},N,0)}}=\frac{\prod\limits_{i=2}^{\ell(\eta)}\prod\limits_{j=0}^{N-i}q_{-i-j+1}^{N+1-i}}{\prod\limits_{j=1}^{N-1}q_{-j}^{N}\cdot\prod\limits_{i=2}^{\ell(\eta)}\prod\limits_{j=1}^{N-i}q_{-i-j+1}^{N+1-i}}=\frac{\prod\limits_{i=1}^{\ell(\eta)-1}q_{-i}^{-i}}{\prod\limits_{j=\ell(\eta)}^{N-1}q_{-j}^{N}}.

∎

Lemma 5.15
.

For any partition
\eta\neq\emptyset
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{q^{\vartheta_{3}(\eta^{c},N,0,1)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{3}(\eta^{r},N,2,1)}}{q^{\vartheta_{3}(\eta,N,1,1)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{3}(\eta^{rc},N,1,1)}}=\left\{\begin{aligned} &q_{\ell(\eta)}^{N-1}\cdot\prod\limits_{i=\ell(\eta)+1}^{N-1}q_{i}^{-1},\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\mbox{if $d(\eta)>1$};\\
&\prod\limits_{i=\ell(\eta)}^{N-1}q_{i}^{-1}\cdot\prod\limits_{i=1}^{\ell(\eta)-1}q_{i}^{-N},\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\mbox{if $d(\eta)=1$ and $\eta_{1}>1$};\\
&\prod\limits_{i=1}^{\ell(\mu)-1}q_{i}^{i}\cdot\prod\limits_{i=\ell(\mu)}^{N-1}q_{i}^{N+i-1},\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\mbox{if $d(\eta)=1$ and $\eta_{1}=1$}.\end{aligned}\right.

Proof.

It follows from the similar argument as in the proof of Lemma
5.13
.
∎

Lemma 5.16
.

For any partition
\eta\neq\emptyset
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{q^{\vartheta_{6}(\eta^{c},N)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{8}(\eta^{r},N+1,1,1)}}{q^{\vartheta_{4}(\eta,N,0)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{4}(\eta^{rc},N,0)}}=\left\{\begin{aligned} &q_{\widetilde{d}(\eta)}^{N}\cdot q_{\ell(\eta)}^{-N}\cdot\prod\limits_{i=\widetilde{d}(\eta)+1}^{\ell(\eta)}\left(\frac{q_{i-1}}{q_{i}}\right)^{\eta_{i}}\cdot\frac{\prod\limits_{i=d(\eta)}^{\widetilde{d}(\eta)-1}q_{i}}{q_{\widetilde{d}(\eta)}^{N+d(\eta)-1}\cdot\prod\limits_{i=\widetilde{d}(\eta)}^{\ell(\eta)-1}q_{i}},\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\mbox{if $d(\eta)>1$};\\
&\prod\limits_{i=1}^{\ell(\eta)-1}q_{i}^{N},\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\mbox{if $d(\eta)=1$ and $\eta_{1}>1$};\\
&\prod\limits_{i=1}^{\ell(\eta)-1}q_{i}^{-i}\cdot\prod\limits_{i=\ell(\eta)}^{N-1}q_{i}^{-N-i},\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\mbox{if $d(\eta)=1$ and $\eta_{1}=1$}.\end{aligned}\right.

Proof.

It follows from the similar argument as in the proof of Lemma
5.14
. ∎

5.4.2.
Weights for the graphical condensation recurrence with
(\mathbf{G}=\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda^{rc},\mu,\nu^{rc}}(N))

By the similar argument in Section 5.4.1, we have

Lemma 5.17
.

The edge-weight of the base
left-up
double-dimer configuration is

\displaystyle q^{\omega_{base}^{LU}(\lambda^{r},\mu,\nu^{c};n)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{N-\ell((\lambda^{r})^{\prime})}\prod_{j=1}^{i}q_{-N+i-j+1}^{i}\cdot\prod_{i:1\leq i\leq(\lambda^{r})^{\prime}_{i}+1}\prod_{j=1}^{N-(\lambda^{r})^{\prime}_{i}}q_{-i-j+2}^{N+(\lambda^{r})^{\prime}_{i}-i+1}\cdot\prod_{i:(\lambda^{r})^{\prime}_{i}+1<i\leq\ell((\lambda^{r})^{\prime})}\prod_{j=1}^{N+1-i}q_{-i-j+2}^{N+(\lambda^{r})^{\prime}_{i}-i+1}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=1}^{N-\ell(\mu)-2}\prod_{j=\ell(\mu)+1}^{N-i-1}q_{i+j}^{N+i}\cdot\prod_{i:1\leq i\leq\mu_{i}}\prod_{j=1}^{N-\mu_{i}-1}q_{i+j}^{N+\mu_{i}+j}\cdot\prod_{i:\mu_{i}<i\leq\ell(\mu)}\prod_{j=1}^{N-i-1}q_{i+j}^{N+\mu_{i}+j}

\displaystyle\times q_{N}^{-N}\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{\ell(\nu^{c})}\prod_{j=0}^{\nu^{c}_{i}-1}q_{j-i+2}^{N-i+1}\cdot\prod_{i=0}^{N}\prod_{j=0}^{N-1}q_{j-i+1}^{N-i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q^{\vartheta_{1}((\lambda^{\prime})^{c},N,0,0)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{5}((\lambda^{\prime})^{c},N+1,1)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{3}(\mu,N-1,-1,0)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{4}(\mu,N,1)}\cdot\left(q^{\varpi_{3}(\nu^{c},N+1,2)}\right)^{-1}\cdot q^{\varpi_{1}(N,1,1)}\cdot q_{N}^{-N}.

Lemma 5.18
.

The edge-weight of the base
right-down
double-dimer configuration is

\displaystyle q^{\omega_{base}^{RD}(\lambda^{c},\mu,\nu^{r};n)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{N-\ell((\lambda^{c})^{\prime})-2}\prod_{j=1}^{i}q_{-N+i-j+1}^{i}\cdot\prod_{i:1\leq i<(\lambda^{c})^{\prime}_{i}-1}\prod_{j=1}^{N-(\lambda^{c})^{\prime}_{i}}q_{-i-j}^{N+(\lambda^{c})^{\prime}_{i}-i-1}\cdot\prod_{i:(\lambda^{c})^{\prime}_{i}-1\leq i\leq\ell((\lambda^{c})^{\prime})}\prod_{j=1}^{N-i-1}q_{-i-j}^{N+(\lambda^{c})^{\prime}_{i}-i-1}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=1}^{N-\ell(\mu)}\prod_{j=\ell(\mu)+1}^{N-i+1}q_{i+j-2}^{N+i-2}\cdot\prod_{i:1\leq i\leq\mu_{i}}\prod_{j=1}^{N-\mu_{i}+1}q_{i+j-2}^{N+\mu_{i}+j-2}\cdot\prod_{i:\mu_{i}<i\leq\ell(\mu)}\prod_{j=1}^{N-i+1}q_{i+j-2}^{N+\mu_{i}+j-2}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=1}^{\ell(\nu^{r})}\prod_{j=0}^{\nu^{r}_{i}-1}q_{j-i}^{N-i-1}\cdot\prod_{i=0}^{N-2}\prod_{j=0}^{N-1}q_{j-i-1}^{N-i-2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q^{\vartheta_{1}((\lambda^{\prime})^{r},N,0,2)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{7}((\lambda^{\prime})^{r},N,0)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{3}(\mu,N+1,3,2)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{4}(\mu,N,-1)}\cdot\left(q^{\varpi_{3}(\nu^{r},N-1,0)}\right)^{-1}\cdot q^{\varpi_{1}(N-2,-1,-1)}.

As in Section 5.4.1, we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda^{rc},\mu,\nu;N)}\cdot q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu,\nu^{rc};N)}}{q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu,\nu;N)}\cdot q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda^{rc},\mu,\nu^{rc};N)}}=1

and still demand the following lemmas to obtain the recurrence (
11
).

Lemma 5.19
.

For any partitions
\lambda,\nu\neq\emptyset
and
\mu
, we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{q^{\omega_{base}^{LU}(\lambda^{r},\mu,\nu^{c};n)}\cdot q^{\omega_{base}^{RD}(\lambda^{c},\mu,\nu^{r};n)}}{q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu,\nu;n)}\cdot q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda^{rc},\mu,\nu^{rc};n)}}=q^{-K_{2}(\lambda,\mu,\nu)}

where
q^{-K_{2}(\lambda,\mu,\nu)}=\left(q^{K_{2}(\lambda,\mu,\nu)}\right)^{-1}
is independent of
N
.

Proof.

It follows from Lemmas
5.8
,
5.17
,
5.18
,
5.20
,
5.21
,
5.22
,
5.23
,

4.30
,
4.27
.
∎

The following lemmas follow from the similar argument in Section 5.4.1.

Lemma 5.20
.

For any partition
\eta\neq\emptyset
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{q^{\vartheta_{1}(\eta^{c},N,0,0)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{1}(\eta^{r},N,0,2)}}{q^{\vartheta_{1}(\eta,N,0,1)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{1}(\eta^{rc},N,0,1)}}=\left\{\begin{aligned} &1,\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\mbox{if $d(\eta)>1$ or $d(\eta)=1$ and $\eta_{1}>1$};\\
&\prod\limits_{i=0}^{N-1}q_{-i}^{N},\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\mbox{if $d(\eta)=1$ and $\eta_{1}=1$}.\end{aligned}\right.

Lemma 5.21
.

For any partition
\eta\neq\emptyset
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{q^{\vartheta_{5}(\eta^{c},N+1,1)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{7}(\eta^{r},N,0)}}{q^{\vartheta_{2}(\eta,N,0)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{2}(\eta^{rc},N,0)}}=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\frac{q_{0}^{N-1}\cdot q_{1-d(\eta)}^{\eta_{d(\eta)}}}{\prod\limits_{i=1}^{d(\eta)-1}q_{-i}}\cdot\prod\limits_{i=1}^{d(\eta)-1}\left(\frac{q_{-i+1}}{q_{-i}}\right)^{\eta_{i}},\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\mbox{if $d(\eta)>1$};\\
&q_{0}^{N+\eta_{1}-1},\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\mbox{if $d(\eta)=1$ and $\eta_{1}>1$};\\
&\prod\limits_{i=1}^{N-1}q_{-i}^{-N},\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\mbox{if $d(\eta)=1$ and $\eta_{1}=1$}.\end{aligned}\right.

Lemma 5.22
.

For any partition
\eta
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{q^{\vartheta_{3}(\eta,N-1,-1,0)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{3}(\eta,N+1,3,2)}}{\left(q^{\vartheta_{3}(\eta,N,1,1)}\right)^{2}}=\frac{q_{\ell(\eta)}^{N-1}}{\prod\limits_{i=\ell(\eta)+1}^{N-1}q_{i}}.

Lemma 5.23
.

For any partition
\eta
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{q^{\vartheta_{4}(\eta,N,1)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{4}(\eta,N,-1)}}{\left(q^{\vartheta_{4}(\eta,N,0)}\right)^{2}}=\left\{\begin{aligned} &1,\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\mbox{if $\eta=\emptyset$};\\
&\frac{q_{0}^{N-1}\cdot q_{\ell(\eta)}^{-N}}{\prod\limits_{i=1}^{\ell(\eta)-1}q_{i}}\cdot\prod\limits_{i=1}^{\ell(\eta)}\left(\frac{q_{i-1}}{q_{i}}\right)^{\eta_{i}},\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\mbox{if $\eta\neq\emptyset$}.\end{aligned}\right.

5.4.3.
Weights for the graphical condensation recurrence with
(\mathbf{G}=\mathbf{H}(N),\mathbf{N}_{\lambda,\mu^{rc},\nu^{rc}}(N))

The similar argument in Section 5.4.1 shows that

Lemma 5.24
.

The edge-weight of the base
left-down
double-dimer configuration is

\displaystyle q^{\omega_{base}^{LD}(\lambda,\mu^{r},\nu^{c};n)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{N-\ell(\lambda^{\prime})}\prod_{j=1}^{i}q_{-N+i-j+1}^{i-1}\cdot\prod_{i:1\leq i\leq\lambda^{\prime}_{i}}\prod_{j=1}^{N-\lambda^{\prime}_{i}+1}q_{-i-j+2}^{N+\lambda^{\prime}_{i}-i}\cdot\prod_{i:\lambda^{\prime}_{i}<i\leq\ell(\lambda^{\prime})}\prod_{j=1}^{N+1-i}q_{-i-j+2}^{N+\lambda^{\prime}_{i}-i}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=1}^{N-\ell(\mu^{r})-2}\prod_{j=\ell(\mu^{r})+1}^{N-i-1}q_{i+j}^{N+i-1}\cdot\prod_{i:1\leq i<\mu^{r}_{i}-1}\prod_{j=1}^{N-\mu^{r}_{i}}q_{i+j}^{N+\mu^{r}_{i}+j-1}\cdot\prod_{i:\mu^{r}_{i}-1\leq i\leq\ell(\mu^{r})}\prod_{j=1}^{N-i-1}q_{i+j}^{N+\mu^{r}_{i}+j-1}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=1}^{\ell(\nu^{c})}\prod_{j=0}^{\nu^{c}_{i}-1}q_{j-i+2}^{N-i}\cdot\prod_{i=0}^{N-1}\prod_{j=0}^{N-2}q_{j-i+1}^{N-i-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q^{\vartheta_{1}(\lambda^{\prime},N,-1,0)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{2}(\lambda^{\prime},N+1,1)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{3}(\mu^{r},N-1,0,0)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{8}(\mu^{r},N,1,0)}\cdot\left(q^{\varpi_{3}(\nu^{c},N,2)}\right)^{-1}\cdot q^{\varpi_{1}(N-1,1,1)}.

Lemma 5.25
.

The edge-weight of the base
right-up
double-dimer configuration is

\displaystyle q^{\omega_{base}^{RU}(\lambda,\mu^{c},\nu^{r};n)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{N-\ell(\lambda^{\prime})-2}\prod_{j=1}^{i}q_{-N+i-j+1}^{i+1}\cdot\prod_{i:1\leq i\leq\lambda^{\prime}_{i}}\prod_{j=1}^{N-\lambda^{\prime}_{i}-1}q_{-i-j}^{N+\lambda^{\prime}_{i}-i}\cdot\prod_{i:\lambda^{\prime}_{i}<i\leq\ell(\lambda^{\prime})}\prod_{j=1}^{N-i-1}q_{-i-j}^{N+\lambda^{\prime}_{i}-i}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=1}^{N-\ell(\mu^{c})}\prod_{j=\ell(\mu^{c})+1}^{N-i+1}q_{i+j-2}^{N+i-1}\cdot\prod_{i:1\leq i<\mu^{c}_{i}+1}\prod_{j=1}^{N-\mu^{c}_{i}}q_{i+j-2}^{N+\mu^{c}_{i}+j-1}\cdot\prod_{i:\mu^{c}_{i}+1\leq i\leq\ell(\mu^{c})}\prod_{j=1}^{N-i+1}q_{i+j-2}^{N+\mu^{c}_{i}+j-1}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=1}^{\ell(\nu^{r})}\prod_{j=0}^{\nu^{r}_{i}-1}q_{j-i}^{N-i}\cdot\prod_{i=0}^{N-1}\prod_{j=0}^{N}q_{j-i-1}^{N-i-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q^{\vartheta_{1}(\lambda^{\prime},N,1,2)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{2}(\lambda^{\prime},N-1,-1)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{3}(\mu^{c},N+1,2,2)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{8}(\mu^{c},N,-1,0)}\cdot\left(q^{\varpi_{3}(\nu^{r},N,0)}\right)^{-1}\cdot q^{\varpi_{1}(N-1,-1,-1)}.

In this case, we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu^{rc},\nu;N)}\cdot q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu,\nu^{rc};nN)}}{q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu,\nu;N)}\cdot q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu^{rc},\nu^{rc};N)}}=1

and still require the following lemmas to achieve Equation (
12
).

Lemma 5.26
.

For any partitions
\mu,\nu\neq\emptyset
and
\lambda
, we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{q^{\omega_{base}^{LD}(\lambda,\mu^{r},\nu^{c};N)}\cdot q^{\omega_{base}^{RU}(\lambda,\mu^{c},\nu^{r};N)}}{q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu,\nu;N)}\cdot q^{\omega_{base}(\lambda,\mu^{rc},\nu^{rc};N)}}=q^{-K_{3}(\lambda,\mu,\nu)}

where
q^{-K_{3}(\lambda,\mu,\nu)}=\left(q^{K_{3}(\lambda,\mu,\nu)}\right)^{-1}
is independent of
N
.

Proof.

It follows from Lemmas
5.8
,
5.24
,
5.25
,

5.27
,
5.28
,

5.29
,
5.30
,
4.35
,
4.34
.
∎

As in Section 5.4.1, we have

Lemma 5.27
.

For any partition
\eta
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{q^{\vartheta_{1}(\eta,N,-1,0)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{1}(\eta,N,1,2)}}{\left(q^{\vartheta_{1}(\eta,N,0,1)}\right)^{2}}=\frac{q_{-\ell(\eta)}^{N-\ell(\eta)-1}}{\prod\limits_{i=1-N}^{-\ell(\eta)-1}q_{i}}.

Lemma 5.28
.

For any partition
\eta
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{q^{\vartheta_{2}(\eta,N+1,1)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{2}(\eta,N-1,-1)}}{\left(q^{\vartheta_{2}(\eta,N,0)}\right)^{2}}=\left\{\begin{aligned} &1,\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\mbox{if $\eta=\emptyset$};\\
&\frac{q_{0}^{N-1}\cdot q_{-\ell(\eta)}^{\ell(\eta)-N}}{\prod\limits_{i=1}^{\ell(\eta)-1}q_{-i}}\cdot\prod\limits_{i=1}^{\ell(\eta)}\left(\frac{q_{-i+1}}{q_{-i}}\right)^{\eta_{i}},\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\mbox{if $\eta\neq\emptyset$}.\end{aligned}\right.

.

For any partition
\eta\neq\emptyset
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{q^{\vartheta_{3}(\eta^{c},N+1,2,2)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{3}(\eta^{r},N-1,0,0)}}{q^{\vartheta_{3}(\eta,N,1,1)}\cdot q^{\vartheta_{3}(\eta^{rc},N,1,1)}}=\left\{\begin{aligned} &1,\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\mbox{if $d(\eta)>1$ or $d(\eta)=1$ and $\eta_{1}>1$};\\
&q_{N-1}^{2N-1}\cdot\prod\limits_{i=0}^{N-2}q_{i}^{N+i},\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\mbox{if $d(\eta)=1$ and $\eta_{1}=1$}.\end{aligned}\right.

.

For any partition

&q_{0}^{N+\eta_{1}-1},\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\mbox{if $d(\eta)=1$ and $\eta_{1}>1$};\\
&\prod\limits_{i=1}^{N-1}q_{i}^{-N-i},\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\mbox{if $d(\eta)=1$ and $\eta_{1}=1$}.\end{aligned}\right.

6.
Orbifold DT/PT correspondence

6.1.
A proof of orbifold DT/PT vertex correspondence

In this subsection, we will give a proof of Conjecture
3.12
and show some applications of orbifold DT/PT vertex correspondence. In fact, the 1-leg correspondence has been proved by explicitly expressing the 1-leg PT
\mathbb{Z}_{n}
-vertex formula in terms of Schur functions in [
45
, Theorem 5.22] via establishing the connection between 1-leg colored labelled box configurations and certain colored reverse plane partitions. Recall that a partition
\eta
is called multi-regular if
|\eta|_{l}=\frac{|\eta|}{n}
for all
l\in\{0,1,\cdots,n-1\}
.

Theorem 6.1
.

([
45
, Theorem 5.22])
If
\nu
is multi-regular, then

\displaystyle\dfrac{V^{n}_{\lambda\emptyset\emptyset}}{V_{\emptyset\emptyset\emptyset}^{n}}=W_{\lambda\emptyset\emptyset}^{n};\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\dfrac{V^{n}_{\emptyset\mu\emptyset}}{V_{\emptyset\emptyset\emptyset}^{n}}=W_{\emptyset\mu\emptyset}^{n};\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\dfrac{V^{n}_{\emptyset\emptyset\nu}}{V_{\emptyset\emptyset\emptyset}^{n}}=W_{\emptyset\emptyset\nu}^{n}.

With the above theorem as the base case, we have

Theorem 6.2
.

If
\nu
is multi-regular, then

\displaystyle V^{n}_{\lambda\mu\nu}=V_{\emptyset\emptyset\emptyset}^{n}W_{\lambda\mu\nu}^{n}.

Proof.

It follows from the recurrences (
7
) and (
10
) with
\nu=\emptyset
, Remark
4.8
, and Theorem
6.1
that

\displaystyle\dfrac{V^{n}_{\lambda\mu\emptyset}}{V_{\emptyset\emptyset\emptyset}^{n}}=W_{\lambda\mu\emptyset}^{n}.

It follows from the recurrences (
8
) and (
11
) with
\mu=\emptyset
, Remark
4.8
, and Theorem
6.1
that

\displaystyle\dfrac{V^{n}_{\lambda\emptyset\nu}}{V_{\emptyset\emptyset\emptyset}^{n}}=W_{\lambda\emptyset\nu}^{n}.

It follows from the recurrences (
9
) and (
12
) with
\lambda=\emptyset
, Remark
4.8
, and Theorem
6.1
that

\displaystyle\dfrac{V^{n}_{\emptyset\mu\nu}}{V_{\emptyset\emptyset\emptyset}^{n}}=W_{\emptyset\mu\nu}^{n}.

Therefore, the proof is completed by the recurrences (
7
) and (
10
), Remark
4.8
, and the 2-leg case just proved above.
∎

Now we recall some notation in [
7
, Section 3.4] as follows. Let
\mathbf{q}_{0}=1
and define
\mathbf{q}_{t}
for
t\in\mathbb{Z}
by the recursive relations
\mathbf{q}_{t}=q_{t}\cdot\mathbf{q}_{t-1}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\{\cdots,\mathbf{q}_{2},\mathbf{q}_{1},\mathbf{q}_{0},\mathbf{q}_{-1},\mathbf{q}_{-2},\cdots\}=\{\cdots,q_{1}q_{2},q_{1},1,q_{0}^{-1},q_{0}^{-1}q_{-1}^{-1},\cdots\}.

For a partition
\eta=(\eta_{0}\geq\eta_{1}\geq\eta_{2}\geq\cdots)
, set

\displaystyle\mathbf{q}_{\bullet-\eta}=\{\mathbf{q}_{-\eta_{0}},\mathbf{q}_{1-\eta_{1}},\mathbf{q}_{2-\eta_{2}},\mathbf{q}_{3-\eta_{3}},\cdots\}.

Let

\displaystyle q^{-A_{\lambda}}:=\prod_{k=0}^{n-1}q_{k}^{-A_{\lambda}(k,n)},

\displaystyle H_{\nu}:=\prod_{(i,j)\in\nu}\frac{1}{1-\prod\limits_{s=0}^{n-1}q_{s}^{h_{\nu}^{s}(i,j)}},

where

\displaystyle h_{\nu}^{s}(i,j):=\mbox{the number of boxes of color s in the $(i,j)$-hook of $\nu$}.

It follows from Theorem
6.2
and [
7
, Theorem 12] that

Corollary 6.3
.

The PT
\mathbb{Z}_{n}
-vertex
W_{\lambda\mu\nu}^{n}(q_{0},q_{1},\cdots,q_{n-1})
has the following explicit formula:

\displaystyle W_{\lambda\mu\nu}^{n}=q^{-A_{\lambda}}\cdot\overline{q^{-A_{\mu^{\prime}}}}\cdot H_{\nu}\cdot\sum_{\eta}q_{0}^{-|\eta|}\cdot\overline{s_{\lambda^{\prime}/\eta}(\mathbf{q}_{\bullet-\nu^{\prime}})}\cdot s_{\mu/\eta}(\mathbf{q}_{\bullet-\nu})

where
\nu
is multi-regular,
s_{\xi/\eta}
is the skew Schur function associated to partitions
\eta\subset\xi
(
s_{\xi/\eta}=0
if
\eta\not\subset\xi
), and the overline denotes the exchange of variables
q_{k}\leftrightarrow q_{-k}
with subscripts in
\mathbb{Z}_{n}
.

Remark 6.4
.

By [
7
, Theorem 12], it is actually proved in [
45
, Theorem 5.22] that for any partition
\nu\neq\emptyset
,

\displaystyle\dfrac{V^{n}_{\emptyset\emptyset\nu}}{O_{\nu}\cdot V_{\emptyset\emptyset\emptyset}^{n}}=W_{\emptyset\emptyset\nu}^{n}

where

\displaystyle O_{\nu}=\prod_{k=0}^{n-1}V_{\emptyset\emptyset\emptyset}^{n}(q_{k},q_{k+1},\cdots,q_{n+k-1})^{-2|\nu|_{k}+|\nu|_{k+1}+|\nu|_{k-1}}.

However, this equality can not be extended to the full 3-leg case in general when
\nu
is not multi-regular and
n>1
. Otherwise both
\dfrac{V^{n}_{\lambda\mu\nu}}{V_{\emptyset\emptyset\emptyset}^{n}}
and
\dfrac{V^{n}_{\lambda\mu\nu}}{O_{\nu}\cdot V_{\emptyset\emptyset\emptyset}^{n}}
(
\nu\neq\emptyset
) will satisfy Equation (
2
) by recurrences (
8
) and (
11
), which implies the equality
O_{\nu}\cdot O_{\nu^{rc}}=O_{\nu^{r}}\cdot O_{\nu^{c}}
. But for any
n\geq 2
, this equality does not hold for the non multi-regular partition
\nu=(1)
.

Remark 6.5
.

It is shown in [
40
, Lemma A.1] that
H_{\nu}
is related to the loop Schur function
\mathfrak{s}_{\nu}
by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{s}_{\nu}(q_{0},q_{1},\cdots,q_{n-1})=\left(\prod_{(i,j)\in\nu}q_{i-j}^{j}\right)\cdot H_{\nu}

where

\displaystyle\mathfrak{s}_{\nu}(q_{0},q_{1},\cdots,q_{n-1}):=\left(\sum_{T\in SSYT(\nu,n)}\prod_{l=0}^{n-1}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}(i,j)\in\nu\\
i-j=l\;mod\;n\end{subarray}}q_{l,\omega((i,j),T)}\right)_{q_{i,j}=q_{i}^{j}}.

Here, the partition
\nu\subset\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{2}
is viewed as a Young diagram in French notation which is different from English notation used for Young diagrams in [
40
]. For each
\nu
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the set
SSYT(\nu,n)
contains semi-standard Young tableaus of the colored Young diagram
\nu
(the box
(i,j)\in\nu
is colored by
i-j\mbox{ mod }n
) whose boxes are numbered such that numbers are weakly increasing from bottom to top and strictly increasing from left to right. For any
T\in SSYT(\nu,n)
, the weight
\omega((i,j),T)
is defined to be the number appearing in the box
(i,j)
. The subscript of the parenthesis above means the substitution
q_{i,j}=q_{i}^{j}
where
i\in\mathbb{Z}_{n}
and
j\in\mathbb{N}
.
One may also refer to [
35
] for loop Schur functions generalizing the classical Schur functions.

Next, we provide a proof of the multi-regular orbifold DT/PT correspondence in [
45
, Conjecture 4.7] for toric CY 3-orbifolds with transverse
A_{n-1}
singularities as follows.

Corollary 6.6
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be a toric CY 3-orbifold with transverse
A_{n-1}
singularities. Then we have

\displaystyle DT^{\prime}_{mr}(\mathcal{X})=PT_{mr}(\mathcal{X}).

Proof.

It follows from Theorem
3.22
, Theorem
6.2
, [
7
, Theorem 10] and the degree 0 DT partition

\displaystyle DT_{0}(\mathcal{X})=\prod_{v\in V_{\mathcal{X}}}V^{n_{e_{3,v}}}_{\emptyset\emptyset\emptyset}(\mathbf{q}_{v})\bigg|_{q_{e_{3,v},0}\to-q_{e_{3,v},0}}

which is obtained by the similar argument in the proof of [
7
, Theorem 10].
∎

Remark 6.7
.

It is observed as in [
40
,
38
] that if we choose for each edge
e

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{C}_{e}}(\mathcal{D}_{e})=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{C}_{e}}(m^{\prime}_{e}p_{e}-\delta_{0,e}^{\prime}p_{0,e}-\delta_{\infty,e}^{\prime}p_{\infty,e}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{C}_{e}}(\mathcal{D}_{e}^{\prime})=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{C}_{e}}(m_{e}p_{e}-\delta_{0,e}p_{0,e}-\delta_{\infty,e}p_{\infty,e})

which are different from the convention in Section 2.1 when
n_{e}=1
, then we have

\displaystyle\underline{PT}_{mr}(\mathcal{X})=\sum_{\{\lambda_{e}\}\in\Lambda_{\mathcal{X}}}\prod_{e\in E_{\mathcal{X}}}\widetilde{E}_{\lambda_{e}}^{e}\prod_{v\in V_{\mathcal{X}}}\widetilde{W}_{\lambda_{1,v}\lambda_{2,v}\lambda_{3,v}}^{n_{e_{3,v}}}(\mathbf{q}_{v})

and

\displaystyle PT_{mr}(\mathcal{X})=\underline{PT}_{mr}(\mathcal{X})\bigg|_{q_{e,0}\to-q_{e,0},\;q\to-q}

where

\displaystyle\widetilde{E}_{\lambda_{e}}^{e}=(-1)^{(m_{e}+\delta_{0,e}+\delta_{\infty,e})|\lambda_{e}|}\cdot\left(\prod_{k=0}^{n_{e}-1}v_{e,k}^{\frac{|\lambda_{e}|}{n_{e}}}\right)\cdot q_{e}^{C_{m_{e}^{\prime},m_{e}}^{\lambda_{e}}},

\displaystyle\widetilde{W}_{\lambda\mu\nu}^{n}=\left(\prod_{(i,j)\in\nu}q_{i-j}^{-j}\right)\cdot\mathfrak{s}_{\nu}\cdot\sum_{\eta}q_{0}^{-|\eta|}\cdot\overline{s_{\lambda^{\prime}/\eta}(\mathbf{q}_{\bullet-\nu^{\prime}})}\cdot s_{\mu/\eta}(\mathbf{q}_{\bullet-\nu}).

6.2.
Remarks on orbifold GW/DT correspondence and DT crepant resolution conjecture

In [
39
,
40
,
37
], the authors establish the orbifold GW/DT correspondence for toric CY 3-orbifolds with transverse
A_{n-1}
singularities by proving orbifold GW/DT vertex correspondence which is compatible with their gluing formulas. As the reduced DT
\mathbb{Z}_{n}
-vertex used in [
39
,
40
,
37
] is the PT
\mathbb{Z}_{n}
-vertex by Theorem
6.2
, the orbifold GW/DT correspondence there is actually the orbifold GW/PT correspondence. Similarly, the proof of DT crepant resolution conjecture for toric CY 3-folds with transverse
A_{n-1}
singularities in [
38
] can be viewed as the validity of the corresponding PT crepant resolution conjecture. Since the orbifold PT theory can be interpreted as a geometric approach to the reduced orbifold DT theory as in the smooth case, we will investigate the orbifold GW/PT correpsondence further as in [
29
,
30
,
31
].
